> DEVELOPER

Investigate GitHub issue #412: "Rust scan keeps stale deleted files and reports clippy-clean imports as unused"

The problem has two parts:
1. After deleting/renaming Rust files, the old paths persist in `state-rust.json` — stale state not cleaned up on rescan
2. Rust `unused` findings contradict a clean `cargo clippy` run

I need to understand:

**For stale files:**
- How does the Rust scan pipeline work? Look at `desloppify/languages/rust/` for the scan entry point
- How does state persistence work? Look at `desloppify/engine/` for how state is written after a scan
- Is there a "prune deleted files" step? How do other languages (Python, TypeScript) handle this?
- Look at how the scan discovers files and how findings get reconciled with existing state

**For unused import false positives:**
- Look at `desloppify/languages/rust/detectors/` for the unused import detector
- How does the Rust unused detector work? Does it shell out to cargo clippy or does it do its own analysis?
- The issue mentions `custom.py` was deleted (see git status: `D desloppify/languages/rust/detectors/custom.py`) — is this related?

**For the `show` command mismatch:**
- The issue says `show orphaned --status open` returns nothing but the issues exist in state. Look at how `show` filters issues.

Search thoroughly across the Rust language plugin, the engine state management, and the scan pipeline. Be very thorough.

> AGENT

I'll investigate this GitHub issue thoroughly. Let me start by exploring the codebase structure to understand the scan pipeline, state persistence, and Rust-specific handling.

    46→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError
    47→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    48→from desloppify.base.search.query import write_query
    49→

    51→
    52→
    53→def _print_scan_header(lang_label: str) -> None:
    54→    """Print the scan header line."""
    55→    print(colorize(f"\nDesloppify Scan{lang_label}\n", "bold"))
    56→
    57→
    58→def _print_scan_complete_banner() -> None:
    59→    """Print scan completion hint banner."""
    60→    lines = [
    61→        colorize("  Scan complete", "bold"),
    62→        colorize("  " + "─" * 50, "dim"),
    63→    ]
    64→    print("\n".join(lines))
    65→
    66→
    67→def _show_scan_visibility(noise, effective_include_slow: bool) -> None:
    68→    """Print fast-scan and noise budget visibility hints.
    69→
    70→    Side-effect only: conditionally prints scan-mode warnings to stdout.
    71→    All branches may be skipped (full scan, no noise budget hit, no hidden
    72→    issues), so the function can legitimately produce no output.
    73→    """
    74→    if not effective_include_slow:
    75→        print(colorize("  * Fast scan — slow phases (duplicates) skipped", "yellow"))
    76→    if noise.budget_warning:
    77→        print(colorize(f"  * {noise.budget_warning}", "yellow"))
    78→    if noise.hidden_total:
    79→        print(
    80→            colorize(
    81→                f"  * {noise.hidden_total} issues hidden (showing {noise.noise_budget}/detector). "
    82→                "Use `desloppify show <detector>` to see all.",
    83→                "dim",
    84→            )
    85→        )
    86→
    87→
    88→def _show_coverage_preflight(runtime) -> None:
    89→    """Print preflight warnings when scan coverage confidence is reduced."""
    90→    warnings = getattr(runtime, "coverage_warnings", []) or []
    91→    if not isinstance(warnings, list) or not warnings:
    92→        return
    93→
    94→    for entry in warnings:
    95→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    96→            continue
    97→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
    98→        impact = str(entry.get("impact", "")).strip()
    99→        remediation = str(entry.get("remediation", "")).strip()
   100→        detector = str(entry.get("detector", "")).strip() or "detector"
   101→
   102→        headline = summary or f"Coverage reduced for `{detector}`."
   103→        print(colorize(f"  * Coverage preflight: {headline}", "yellow"))
   104→        if impact:
   105→            print(colorize(f"    Repercussion: {impact}", "dim"))
   106→        if remediation:
   107→            print(colorize(f"    Fix: {remediation}", "dim"))
   108→
   109→
   110→def _print_plan_workflow_nudge(state: dict) -> None:
   111→    _print_plan_workflow_nudge_impl(state)
   112→
   113→
   114→def cmd_scan(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   115→    """Run all detectors, update persistent state, show diff."""
   116→    scan_preflight_mod.scan_queue_preflight(args)
   117→    try:
   118→        runtime = prepare_scan_runtime(args)
   119→    except LangRuntimeOptionsError as exc:
   120→        lang_cfg = resolve_lang(args)
   121→        lang_name = lang_cfg.name if lang_cfg else "selected"
   122→        print_lang_runtime_options_error(exc, lang_name=lang_name)
   123→        raise CommandError(str(exc), exit_code=2) from exc
   124→    except ScanStateContractError as exc:
   125→        raise CommandError(str(exc), exit_code=2) from exc
   126→    orchestrator = ScanOrchestrator(
   127→        runtime,
   128→        run_scan_generation_fn=run_scan_generation,
   129→        merge_scan_results_fn=merge_scan_results,
   130→        resolve_noise_snapshot_fn=resolve_noise_snapshot,
   131→        persist_reminder_history_fn=persist_reminder_history,
   132→    )
   133→    _print_scan_header(runtime.lang_label)
   134→    if runtime.reset_subjective_count > 0:
   135→        print(
   136→            colorize(
   137→                "  * Subjective reset "
   138→                f"{runtime.reset_subjective_count} subjective dimensions to 0",
   139→                "yellow",
   140→            )
   141→        )
   142→    _show_coverage_preflight(runtime)
   143→
   144→    issues, potentials, codebase_metrics = orchestrator.generate()
   145→    merge = orchestrator.merge(issues, potentials, codebase_metrics)
   146→    _print_scan_complete_banner()
   147→
   148→    noise = orchestrator.noise_snapshot()
   149→
   150→    target_value = target_strict_score_from_config(runtime.config)
   151→
   152→    show_diff_summary(merge.diff)
   153→    show_score_delta(
   154→        runtime.state,
   155→        merge.prev_overall,
   156→        merge.prev_objective,
   157→        merge.prev_strict,
   158→        merge.prev_verified,
   159→        target_strict=target_value,
   160→    )
   161→    # Nudge: if plan_start_scores was just seeded, tell the agent about the lifecycle.
   162→    _print_plan_workflow_nudge(runtime.state)
   163→    _show_scan_visibility(noise, runtime.effective_include_slow)
   164→    show_scorecard_subjective_measures(runtime.state)
   165→    show_score_model_breakdown(runtime.state)
   166→
   167→    new_dim_scores = runtime.state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   168→    if new_dim_scores and merge.prev_dim_scores:
   169→        show_dimension_deltas(merge.prev_dim_scores, new_dim_scores)
   170→
   171→    warnings, narrative = show_post_scan_analysis(
   172→        merge.diff,
   173→        runtime.state,
   174→        runtime.lang,
   175→        target_strict_score=target_value,
   176→    )
   177→    orchestrator.persist_reminders(narrative)
   178→
   179→    write_query(
   180→        build_scan_query_payload(
   181→            runtime.state,
   182→            runtime.config,
   183→            runtime.profile,
   184→            merge.diff,
   185→            warnings,
   186→            narrative,
   187→            merge,
   188→            noise,
   189→        ),
   190→        query_file=query_file_path(),
   191→    )
   192→
   193→    badge_path, _badge_result = emit_scorecard_badge(args, runtime.config, runtime.state)
   194→    print_llm_summary(runtime.state, badge_path, narrative, merge.diff)
   195→    auto_update_skill()
   196→
   197→
   198→__all__ = [
   199→    "cmd_scan",
   200→]
   201→

1→"""Scan lifecycle orchestration service.
     2→
     3→Keeps scan command flow explicit while delegating stage implementations to
     4→scan_workflow helpers.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from collections.abc import Callable
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.scan.workflow import (
    14→    ScanMergeResult,
    15→    ScanNoiseSnapshot,
    16→    ScanRuntime,
    17→    merge_scan_results,
    18→    persist_reminder_history,
    19→    resolve_noise_snapshot,
    20→    run_scan_generation,
    21→)
    22→
    23→
    24→@dataclass
    25→class ScanOrchestrator:
    26→    """Compose scan lifecycle stages around a resolved ScanRuntime."""
    27→
    28→    runtime: ScanRuntime
    29→    run_scan_generation_fn: Callable[
    30→        [ScanRuntime],
    31→        tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, object], dict[str, object] | None],
    32→    ] = run_scan_generation
    33→    merge_scan_results_fn: Callable[
    34→        [ScanRuntime, list[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, object], dict[str, object] | None],
    35→        ScanMergeResult,
    36→    ] = merge_scan_results
    37→    resolve_noise_snapshot_fn: Callable[
    38→        [dict[str, Any], dict[str, object]],
    39→        ScanNoiseSnapshot,
    40→    ] = resolve_noise_snapshot
    41→    persist_reminder_history_fn: Callable[
    42→        [ScanRuntime, dict[str, object]],
    43→        None,
    44→    ] = persist_reminder_history
    45→
    46→    def generate(
    47→        self,
    48→    ) -> tuple[list[dict[str, object]], dict[str, object], dict[str, object] | None]:
    49→        """Run detector generation and lifecycle augmenters."""
    50→        return self.run_scan_generation_fn(self.runtime)
    51→
    52→    def merge(
    53→        self,
    54→        issues: list[dict[str, object]],
    55→        potentials: dict[str, object],
    56→        codebase_metrics: dict[str, object] | None,
    57→    ) -> ScanMergeResult:
    58→        """Merge generated issues and persist scan state updates."""
    59→        return self.merge_scan_results_fn(
    60→            self.runtime,
    61→            issues,
    62→            potentials,
    63→            codebase_metrics,
    64→        )
    65→
    66→    def noise_snapshot(self) -> ScanNoiseSnapshot:
    67→        """Resolve effective noise budgets for display/query payloads."""
    68→        return self.resolve_noise_snapshot_fn(self.runtime.state, self.runtime.config)
    69→
    70→    def persist_reminders(self, narrative: dict[str, object]) -> None:
    71→        """Persist reminder history emitted by post-scan narrative analysis."""
    72→        self.persist_reminder_history_fn(self.runtime, narrative)
    73→
    74→
    75→__all__ = ["ScanOrchestrator"]
    76→

    99→    if isinstance(assessments, dict):
   100→        return assessments
   101→    raise ScanStateContractError(
   102→        "state.subjective_assessments must be an object; rerun with a valid state file"
   103→    )

   105→
   106→def _ensure_state_lang_capabilities(
   107→    state: StateModel,
   108→) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
   109→    """Return language capability map, creating storage when missing."""
   110→    capabilities = state.get("lang_capabilities")
   111→    if capabilities is None:
   112→        normalized: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = {}
   113→        state["lang_capabilities"] = normalized
   114→        return normalized
   115→    if isinstance(capabilities, dict):
   116→        return capabilities
   117→    raise ScanStateContractError(
   118→        "state.lang_capabilities must be an object when present"
   119→    )
   120→
   121→
   122→def _state_issues(state: StateModel) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
   123→    """Return normalized issue map from state."""
   124→    issues = state.get("work_items")
   125→    if isinstance(issues, dict):

   134→    return resettable_default_dimensions(lang_name=lang_name)

Review PR #413: "Fix planner deadlock when triage goes stale mid-cycle"

The PR description says: when triage is stale but deferred behind objective backlog, both `plan resolve --confirm` and `plan triage --run-stages` were blocked — a deadlock. The fix allows objective resolves to continue while keeping direct review resolves blocked.

I need to understand the changes and whether they're correct. Please:

1. Get the full diff: run `gh pr diff 413`
2. Read the key files being modified to understand the context
3. Check if the tests listed in the PR actually pass: look at what test files are added/modified
4. Look for any issues: edge cases, missed spots, inconsistencies

Focus on understanding:
- The resolve guard logic and how "pending" vs "blocked" states differ
- Whether the banner/lifecycle copy changes are consistent
- Whether the plan reloading optimization (passing already-loaded plan) is safe
- Whether test coverage is adequate for the new behavior

Be very thorough in reading the diff and the surrounding code.

   212→def _apply_assessment_reset(payload: dict, *, source: str, now: str) -> None:
   213→    """Apply a standard reset mutation to a single subjective assessment payload.
   214→
   215→    Sets score to 0.0, stamps assessed_at/reset_by/placeholder, and strips
   216→    any cached scoring artifacts (integrity_penalty, components,
   217→    component_scores) plus provisional-override markers.
   218→    """
   219→    payload["score"] = 0.0
   220→    payload["source"] = source
   221→    payload["assessed_at"] = now
   222→    payload["reset_by"] = source
   223→    payload["placeholder"] = True
   224→    payload.pop("integrity_penalty", None)
   225→    payload.pop("components", None)
   226→    payload.pop("component_scores", None)
   227→    payload.pop("provisional_override", None)

   231→def _reset_subjective_assessments_for_scan_reset(
   232→    state: StateModel,
   233→    *,
   234→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   235→) -> int:
   236→    """Reset known subjective dimensions to 0 so the next scan starts fresh."""
   237→    assessments = _state_subjective_assessments(state)
   238→
   239→    reset_keys = {
   240→        key.strip()
   241→        for key in assessments
   242→        if isinstance(key, str) and key.strip()
   243→    }
   244→    reset_keys.update(_subjective_reset_dimensions(lang_name=lang_name))
   245→
   246→    now = utc_now()
   247→    source = "scan_reset_subjective"
   248→    for key in sorted(reset_keys):
   249→        payload = assessments.get(key)
   250→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
   251→            _apply_assessment_reset(payload, source=source, now=now)
   252→            continue
   253→        assessments[key] = {
   254→            "score": 0.0,
   255→            "source": source,
   256→            "assessed_at": now,
   257→            "reset_by": source,
   258→            "placeholder": True,
   259→        }
   260→    return len(reset_keys)

   267→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   268→    state_file = runtime.state_path

diff --git a/desloppify/tests/commands/helpers/test_guardrails.py b/desloppify/tests/commands/helpers/test_guardrails.py
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..f0916bed
--- /dev/null
+++ b/desloppify/tests/commands/helpers/test_guardrails.py
@@ -0,0 +1,93 @@
+"""Regression tests for triage guardrails around deferred mid-cycle triage."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import pytest
+
+from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.guardrails import (
+    require_triage_current_or_exit,
+    triage_guardrail_messages,
+    triage_guardrail_status,
+)
+from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError
+from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan
+
+
+def _pending_triage_plan() -> dict:
+    plan = empty_plan()
+    plan["plan_start_scores"] = {"strict": 72.0}
+    plan["epic_triage_meta"] = {"triaged_ids": ["review::old"]}
+    return plan
+
+
+def _pending_triage_state() -> dict:
+    return {
+        "issues": {
+            "obj::1": {
+                "id": "obj::1",
+                "status": "open",
+                "detector": "complexity",
+                "summary": "Objective work still open",
+            },
+            "review::old": {
+                "id": "review::old",
+                "status": "open",
+                "detector": "review",
+                "summary": "Previously triaged review issue",
+                "detail": {"dimension": "naming"},
+            },
+            "review::new": {
+                "id": "review::new",
+                "status": "open",
+                "detector": "review",
+                "summary": "New review issue",
+                "detail": {"dimension": "naming"},
+            },
+        }
+    }
+
+
+def test_triage_guardrail_status_marks_pending_behind_objective_backlog() -> None:
+    result = triage_guardrail_status(
+        plan=_pending_triage_plan(),
+        state=_pending_triage_state(),
+    )
+
+    assert result.is_stale is True
+    assert result.pending_behind_objective_backlog is True
+    assert result.new_ids == {"review::new"}
+
+
+def test_triage_guardrail_messages_use_pending_copy_when_triage_is_deferred() -> None:
+    messages = triage_guardrail_messages(
+        plan=_pending_triage_plan(),
+        state=_pending_triage_state(),
+    )
+
+    assert any("activate after the current objective backlog is clear" in msg for msg in messages)
+    assert any(msg.startswith("TRIAGE PENDING") for msg in messages)
+    assert not any("Run the staged triage runner" in msg for msg in messages)
+
+
+def test_require_triage_current_allows_objective_resolve_while_pending(capsys) -> None:
+    require_triage_current_or_exit(
+        state=_pending_triage_state(),
+        plan=_pending_triage_plan(),
+        patterns=["obj::1"],
+        attest="",
+    )
+
+    out = capsys.readouterr().out
+    assert "TRIAGE PENDING" in out
+
+
+def test_require_triage_current_blocks_review_resolve_while_pending() -> None:
+    with pytest.raises(CommandError) as exc_info:
+        require_triage_current_or_exit(
+            state=_pending_triage_state(),
+            plan=_pending_triage_plan(),
+            patterns=["review::new"],
+            attest="",
+        )
+
+    assert "triage is pending behind the current objective backlog" in str(exc_info.value)

+def test_banner_pending_when_stale_triage_is_deferred_behind_objective_backlog():
+    plan = empty_plan()
+    plan["plan_start_scores"] = {"strict": 72.0}
+    plan["epic_triage_meta"] = {"triaged_ids": ["review::old"]}
+    state = {
+        "issues": {
+            "obj-1": {"id": "obj-1", "status": "open", "detector": "complexity"},
+            "review::old": {"id": "review::old", "status": "open", "detector": "review"},
+            "review::new": {"id": "review::new", "status": "open", "detector": "review"},
+        }
+    }
+
+    banner = triage_phase_banner(plan, state)
+    assert banner.startswith("TRIAGE PENDING")

```
1→"""Shared helpers for queue-sync decisions.
     2→
     3→Every sync module (``sync_triage``, ``sync_dimensions``, ``sync_workflow``,
     4→``auto_cluster_sync``) needs to answer two questions:
     5→
     6→1. **Is there an objective backlog?**  (Should we defer optional items?)
     7→2. **Are we mid-cycle?**  (Has a queue cycle started and not yet completed?)
     8→
     9→This module provides a single definition of each so the logic stays
    10→consistent and changes propagate everywhere at once.
    11→"""
    12→
    13→from __future__ import annotations
    14→
    15→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel
    16→from desloppify.engine._plan.policy.subjective import (
    17→    NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS as _NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.engine._plan.policy.subjective import (
    20→    SubjectiveVisibility,
    21→)
    22→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    23→
    24→
    25→def has_objective_backlog(
    26→    state_or_issues: StateModel | dict,
    27→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None,
    28→) -> bool:
    29→    """Return whether a planned objective backlog exists.
    30→
    31→    Prefers the pre-computed *policy* snapshot when available (plan-aware:
    32→    only counts planned objectives post-triage).  Falls back to scanning
    33→    *state_or_issues* directly (counts all open non-subjective issues).
    34→    """
    35→    if policy is not None:
    36→        return policy.has_objective_backlog
    37→
    38→    # Accept either full state payload (`{"work_items": ...}`) or a raw
    39→    # work-item mapping, with legacy ``issues`` as a fallback alias.
    40→    issues = state_or_issues
    41→    if isinstance(state_or_issues, dict):
    42→        maybe_issues = state_or_issues.get("work_items")
    43→        if not isinstance(maybe_issues, dict):
    44→            maybe_issues = state_or_issues.get("issues")
    45→        if isinstance(maybe_issues, dict):
    46→            issues = maybe_issues
    47→    return any(
    48→        f.get("status") == "open"
    49→        and f.get("detector") not in _NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
    50→        and not f.get("suppressed")
    51→        for f in issues.values()
    52→    )
    53→
    54→
    55→def is_mid_cycle(plan: PlanModel) -> bool:
    56→    """True when a queue cycle is in progress.
    57→
    58→    A cycle is active when ``plan_start_scores`` is set to real score
    59→    values.  The ``{"reset": True}`` sentinel (set by lifecycle reset)
    60→    does NOT count as mid-cycle — it means "seed real scores on next scan".
    61→    """
    62→    scores = plan.get("plan_start_scores")
    63→    return bool(scores) and not (isinstance(scores, dict) and scores.get("reset"))
    64→
    65→
    66→__all__ = ["has_objective_backlog", "is_mid_cycle"]
    67→
```

1→"""Shared triage guardrail helpers for command entrypoints."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     7→
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.issue_id_display import short_issue_id
     9→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS, CommandError
    10→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    11→from desloppify.engine.plan_state import load_plan
    12→from desloppify.engine.plan_triage import (
    13→    TRIAGE_CMD_RUN_STAGES_CLAUDE,
    14→    TRIAGE_CMD_RUN_STAGES_CODEX,
    15→    TriageSnapshot,
    16→    build_triage_snapshot,
    17→    triage_phase_banner,
    18→)
    19→
    20→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→
    23→@dataclass
    24→class TriageGuardrailResult:
    25→    """Structured result from triage staleness detection."""
    26→
    27→    is_stale: bool = False
    28→    new_ids: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
    29→    _plan: dict | None = field(default=None, repr=False)
    30→    _snapshot: TriageSnapshot | None = field(default=None, repr=False)
    31→
    32→
    33→def triage_guardrail_status(
    34→    *,
    35→    plan: dict | None = None,
    36→    state: dict | None = None,
    37→) -> TriageGuardrailResult:
    38→    """Pure detection: is triage stale? Returns structured result, no side effects."""
    39→    try:
    40→        resolved_plan = plan if isinstance(plan, dict) else load_plan()
    41→    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
    42→        logger.debug("Triage guardrail status skipped: plan could not be loaded.", exc_info=exc)
    43→        return TriageGuardrailResult()
    44→
    45→    resolved_state = state or {}
    46→
    47→    snapshot = build_triage_snapshot(resolved_plan, resolved_state)
    48→    if not snapshot.is_triage_stale:
    49→        return TriageGuardrailResult(_plan=resolved_plan, _snapshot=snapshot)
    50→
    51→    return TriageGuardrailResult(
    52→        is_stale=True,
    53→        new_ids=set(snapshot.new_since_triage_ids),
    54→        _plan=resolved_plan,
    55→        _snapshot=snapshot,
    56→    )
    57→
    58→
    59→def triage_guardrail_messages(
    60→    *,
    61→    plan: dict | None = None,
    62→    state: dict | None = None,
    63→) -> list[str]:
    64→    """Return warning strings without printing."""
    65→    resolved_state = state or {}
    66→    result = triage_guardrail_status(plan=plan, state=state)
    67→    if not result.is_stale:
    68→        return []
    69→
    70→    messages: list[str] = []
    71→    if result.new_ids:
    72→        messages.append(
    73→            f"{len(result.new_ids)} new review work item(s) not yet triaged."
    74→            " Run the staged triage runner to incorporate them "
    75→            f"(`{TRIAGE_CMD_RUN_STAGES_CODEX}` or `{TRIAGE_CMD_RUN_STAGES_CLAUDE}`)."
    76→        )
    77→
    78→    if result._plan is not None:
    79→        banner = triage_phase_banner(result._plan, resolved_state, snapshot=result._snapshot)
    80→        if banner:
    81→            messages.append(banner)
    82→
    83→    return messages
    84→
    85→
    86→def print_triage_guardrail_info(
    87→    *,
    88→    plan: dict | None = None,
    89→    state: dict | None = None,
    90→) -> bool:
    91→    """Print yellow info banner if triage is stale. Returns True if banner was shown."""
    92→    messages = triage_guardrail_messages(plan=plan, state=state)
    93→    for msg in messages:
    94→        print(colorize(f"  {msg}", "yellow"))
    95→    return bool(messages)
    96→
    97→
    98→def require_triage_current_or_exit(
    99→    *,
   100→    state: dict,
   101→    bypass: bool = False,
   102→    attest: str = "",
   103→) -> None:
   104→    """Gate: exit(1) if triage is stale and not bypassed. Name signals the exit."""
   105→    result = triage_guardrail_status(state=state)
   106→    if not result.is_stale:
   107→        return
   108→
   109→    if bypass and attest and len(attest.strip()) >= 30:
   110→        print(colorize(
   111→            "  Triage guardrail bypassed with attestation.",
   112→            "yellow",
   113→        ))
   114→        return
   115→
   116→    new_ids = result.new_ids
   117→    lines = [
   118→        f"BLOCKED: {len(new_ids) or 'some'} new review work item(s) have not been triaged."
   119→    ]
   120→    if new_ids:
   121→        for fid in sorted(new_ids)[:5]:
   122→            issue = (state.get("work_items") or state.get("issues", {})).get(fid, {})
   123→            lines.append(f"    * [{short_issue_id(fid)}] {issue.get('summary', '')}")
   124→        if len(new_ids) > 5:
   125→            lines.append(f"    ... and {len(new_ids) - 5} more")
   126→    lines.append("")
   127→    lines.append(f"  NEXT STEP (Codex):  {TRIAGE_CMD_RUN_STAGES_CODEX}")
   128→    lines.append(f"  NEXT STEP (Claude): {TRIAGE_CMD_RUN_STAGES_CLAUDE}")
   129→    lines.append("  Manual fallback:    desloppify plan triage")
   130→    lines.append("  (Review new issues, then either --confirm-existing or re-plan.)")
   131→    lines.append("")
   132→    lines.append("  View new execution items:  desloppify plan queue --sort recent")
   133→    lines.append("  View broader backlog:      desloppify backlog")
   134→    lines.append('  To bypass: --force-resolve --attest "I understand the plan may be stale..."')
   135→    raise CommandError("\n".join(lines))
   136→
   137→
   138→__all__ = [
   139→    "TriageGuardrailResult",
   140→    "print_triage_guardrail_info",
   141→    "require_triage_current_or_exit",
   142→    "triage_guardrail_messages",
   143→    "triage_guardrail_status",
   144→]
   145→

    40→
    41→def _mark_stale_on_mechanical_change(
    42→    state: StateModel,
    43→    *,
    44→    changed_detectors: set[str],
    45→    now: str,
    46→) -> None:
    47→    """Mark subjective assessments stale when mechanical issues change.
    48→
    49→    Only marks dimensions that already have an assessment — doesn't create
    50→    new entries for dimensions that have never been reviewed.
    51→    """
    52→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    53→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    54→        return
    55→
    56→    affected_dims: set[str] = set()
    57→    for detector in changed_detectors:
    58→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
    59→        if meta is None or not meta.marks_dims_stale:
    60→            continue
    61→        det_meta = get_detector_meta(detector)
    62→        dims = det_meta.subjective_dimensions if det_meta else ()
    63→        if dims:
    64→            affected_dims.update(dims)
    65→            continue
    66→        # Safety fallback for newly added "marks_dims_stale" detectors that
    67→        # have not declared fine-grained dimension mappings yet.
    68→        affected_dims.update(
    69→            dim
    70→            for dim in assessments
    71→            if isinstance(dim, str) and dim.strip()
    72→        )
    73→
    74→    if not affected_dims:
    75→        return
    76→
    77→    for dimension in sorted(affected_dims):
    78→        if dimension not in assessments:
    79→            continue
    80→        payload = assessments[dimension]
    81→        if not isinstance(payload, dict):
    82→            continue
    83→        # Don't overwrite if already stale
    84→        if payload.get("needs_review_refresh"):
    85→            continue
    86→        payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    87→        payload["refresh_reason"] = "mechanical_issues_changed"
    88→        payload["stale_since"] = now
    89→

1→"""Issue upsert/verification helpers for scan merge."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import matches_exclusion
     6→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import matched_ignore_pattern
     7→from desloppify.engine._state.issue_semantics import (
     8→    is_import_only_issue,
     9→    is_assessment_request,
    10→)
    11→
    12→
    13→def find_suspect_detectors(
    14→    existing: dict,
    15→    current_by_detector: dict[str, int],
    16→    force_resolve: bool,
    17→    ran_detectors: set[str] | None = None,
    18→) -> set[str]:
    19→    """Detectors that had open issues but likely did not actually run this scan."""
    20→    if force_resolve:
    21→        return set()
    22→
    23→    previous_open_by_detector: dict[str, int] = {}
    24→    for issue in existing.values():
    25→        if issue["status"] != "open":
    26→            continue
    27→        detector = issue.get("detector", "unknown")
    28→        previous_open_by_detector[detector] = (
    29→            previous_open_by_detector.get(detector, 0) + 1
    30→        )
    31→
    32→    suspect: set[str] = {
    33→        str(issue.get("detector", "unknown"))
    34→        for issue in existing.values()
    35→        if isinstance(issue, dict) and is_import_only_issue(issue)
    36→    }
    37→
    38→    for detector, previous_count in previous_open_by_detector.items():
    39→        if detector in suspect:
    40→            continue
    41→        if current_by_detector.get(detector, 0) > 0:
    42→            continue
    43→        if ran_detectors is not None:
    44→            if detector not in ran_detectors:
    45→                suspect.add(detector)
    46→            continue
    47→        if previous_count >= 3:
    48→            suspect.add(detector)
    49→
    50→    return suspect
    51→
    52→
    53→def _mark_scan_verified(
    54→    issue: dict,
    55→    now: str,
    56→    *,
    57→    note: str,
    58→    attestation_text: str,
    59→) -> None:
    60→    """Record scan corroboration without changing the manual disposition."""
    61→    issue["suppressed"] = False
    62→    issue["suppressed_at"] = None
    63→    issue["suppression_pattern"] = None
    64→    issue["note"] = note
    65→    existing = issue.get("resolution_attestation")
    66→    if not isinstance(existing, dict):
    67→        existing = {}
    68→        issue["resolution_attestation"] = existing
    69→    existing["scan_verified"] = True
    70→    existing["scan_verified_at"] = now
    71→    existing["scan_verification_text"] = attestation_text
    72→
    73→
    74→def verify_disappeared(
    75→    existing: dict,
    76→    current_ids: set[str],
    77→    suspect_detectors: set[str],
    78→    now: str,
    79→    *,
    80→    lang: str | None,
    81→    scan_path: str | None,
    82→    exclude: tuple[str, ...] = (),
    83→) -> tuple[int, int, int, set[str]]:
    84→    """Update scan corroboration for issues absent from scan.
    85→
    86→    Returns (resolved_count, skipped_other_lang, resolved_out_of_scope, changed_detectors).
    87→    Queue-tracked work stays user-controlled: disappearing from scan does not
    88→    change an open issue to resolved. Manually resolved items can be marked as
    89→    scan-verified when they remain absent.
    90→    """
    91→    resolved = skipped_other_lang = resolved_out_of_scope = 0
    92→    resolved_detectors: set[str] = set()
    93→
    94→    for issue_id, previous in existing.items():
    95→        previous_status = previous.get("status")
    96→        if issue_id in current_ids or previous_status not in (
    97→            "open",
    98→            "wontfix",
    99→            "fixed",
   100→            "false_positive",
   101→        ):
   102→            continue
   103→
   104→        if lang and previous.get("lang") and previous["lang"] != lang:
   105→            skipped_other_lang += 1
   106→            continue
   107→
   108→        # Suspect detectors (e.g. 'review') are import-only and must never
   109→        # be auto-resolved by a scan — check this BEFORE the scope filter
   110→        # so that review issues with file="." aren't wrongly resolved as
   111→        # "out of current scan scope".
   112→        if previous.get("detector", "unknown") in suspect_detectors:
   113→            continue
   114→
   115→        if scan_path and scan_path != ".":
   116→            prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
   117→            if (
   118→                not previous["file"].startswith(prefix)
   119→                and previous["file"] != scan_path
   120→            ):
   121→                if previous_status != "open":
   122→                    scope_note = f"Still absent in current scan scope ({scan_path})"
   123→                    _mark_scan_verified(
   124→                        previous,
   125→                        now,
   126→                        note=scope_note,
   127→                        attestation_text=scope_note,
   128→                    )
   129→                    resolved_detectors.add(previous.get("detector", "unknown"))
   130→                    resolved_out_of_scope += 1
   131→                continue
   132→
   133→        if exclude and any(matches_exclusion(previous["file"], ex) for ex in exclude):
   134→            continue
   135→
   136→        if previous_status == "open":
   137→            continue
   138→
   139→        verification_note = (
   140→            "Still absent from scan after manual wontfix"
   141→            if previous_status == "wontfix"
   142→            else "Still absent from scan after manual resolution"
   143→        )
   144→        _mark_scan_verified(
   145→            previous,
   146→            now,
   147→            note=verification_note,
   148→            attestation_text="Absent from detector output in latest scan",
   149→        )
   150→        resolved_detectors.add(previous.get("detector", "unknown"))
   151→        resolved += 1
   152→
   153→    return resolved, skipped_other_lang, resolved_out_of_scope, resolved_detectors
   154→
   155→
   156→def upsert_issues(
   157→    existing: dict,
   158→    current_issues: list[dict],
   159→    ignore: list[str],
   160→    now: str,
   161→    *,
   162→    lang: str | None,
   163→) -> tuple[set[str], int, int, dict[str, int], int, set[str]]:
   164→    """Insert new issues and update existing ones.
   165→
   166→    Returns (current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, by_detector, ignored_count, changed_detectors).
   167→    """
   168→    current_ids: set[str] = set()
   169→    new_count = reopened_count = ignored_count = 0
   170→    by_detector: dict[str, int] = {}
   171→    changed_detectors: set[str] = set()
   172→
   173→    for issue in current_issues:
   174→        issue_id = issue["id"]
   175→        detector = issue.get("detector", "unknown")
   176→        current_ids.add(issue_id)
   177→        by_detector[detector] = by_detector.get(detector, 0) + 1
   178→        matched_ignore = matched_ignore_pattern(issue_id, issue["file"], ignore)
   179→        if matched_ignore:
   180→            ignored_count += 1
   181→
   182→        if lang:
   183→            issue["lang"] = lang
   184→
   185→        if issue_id not in existing:
   186→            existing[issue_id] = dict(issue)
   187→            if matched_ignore:
   188→                existing[issue_id]["suppressed"] = True
   189→                existing[issue_id]["suppressed_at"] = now
   190→                existing[issue_id]["suppression_pattern"] = matched_ignore
   191→                continue
   192→            new_count += 1
   193→            changed_detectors.add(detector)
   194→            continue
   195→
   196→        previous = existing[issue_id]
   197→        previous.update(
   198→            last_seen=now,
   199→            tier=issue["tier"],
   200→            confidence=issue["confidence"],

1→"""State resolution operations (match + resolve)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import copy
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "coerce_assessment_score",
     9→    "match_issues",

    13→from desloppify.base.text_utils import is_numeric
    14→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import _matches_pattern
    15→from desloppify.engine._state.issue_semantics import is_review_finding
    16→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    17→    StateModel,
    18→    ensure_state_defaults,

    21→)

    24→from desloppify.engine._state import _recompute_stats
    25→
    26→
    27→def _preserve_integrity_target(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
    28→    """Extract the current subjective integrity target so recompute doesn't erase it."""
    29→    integrity = state.get("subjective_integrity")
    30→    if not isinstance(integrity, dict):
    31→        return None
    32→    raw = integrity.get("target_score")
    33→    if raw is None:
    34→        return None
    35→    try:
    36→        return max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw)))
    37→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
    38→        return None
    39→
    40→
    41→def coerce_assessment_score(value: object) -> float | None:
    42→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float.
    43→
    44→    Returns ``None`` when the value cannot be interpreted as a numeric score
    45→    (e.g. bools, non-numeric strings, missing keys).
    46→    """
    47→    if is_numeric(value):
    48→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value))), 1)
    49→    if isinstance(value, dict):
    50→        raw = value.get("score")
    51→        if not is_numeric(raw):
    52→            return None
    53→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw))), 1)
    54→    return None
    55→
    56→
    57→def _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
    58→    state: StateModel,
    59→    *,
    60→    status: str,
    61→    resolved_issues: list[dict],
    62→    now: str,
    63→) -> None:
    64→    """Mark subjective assessments as stale when review issues are resolved.
    65→
    66→    The assessment score is preserved (not zeroed) — only a fresh review import
    67→    should change dimension scores.  The stale marker tells the UI to prompt
    68→    for a re-review.
    69→    """
    70→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    71→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    72→        return
    73→
    74→    touched_dimensions: set[str] = set()
    75→    for issue in resolved_issues:
    76→        if not is_review_finding(issue):
    77→            continue
    78→        dimension = str(issue.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).strip()
    79→        if dimension:
    80→            touched_dimensions.add(dimension)
    81→
    82→    for dimension in sorted(touched_dimensions):
    83→        if dimension not in assessments:
    84→            continue
    85→
    86→        payload = assessments.get(dimension)
    87→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
    88→            payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    89→            payload["refresh_reason"] = f"review_issue_{status}"
    90→            payload["stale_since"] = now
    91→        else:
    92→            assessments[dimension] = {
    93→                "score": coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0,
    94→                "needs_review_refresh": True,
    95→                "refresh_reason": f"review_issue_{status}",
    96→                "stale_since": now,
    97→            }
    98→
    99→
   100→def match_issues(
   101→    state: StateModel, pattern: str, status_filter: str = "open"
   102→) -> list[dict]:
   103→    """Return issues matching *pattern* with the given status."""
   104→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   105→    return [
   106→        issue
   107→        for issue_id, issue in state["work_items"].items()
   108→        if not issue.get("suppressed")
   109→        if (status_filter == "all" or issue["status"] == status_filter)
   110→        and _matches_pattern(issue_id, issue, pattern)
   111→    ]
   112→
   113→
   114→def resolve_issues(
   115→    state: StateModel,
   116→    pattern: str,
   117→    status: str,
   118→    note: str | None = None,
   119→    attestation: str | None = None,
   120→) -> list[str]:
   121→    """Set issue status for matches and return affected issue IDs."""
   122→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   123→    now = utc_now()
   124→    resolved: list[str] = []
   125→    resolved_issues: list[dict] = []
   126→
   127→    def _refresh_original_wontfix(issue: dict) -> None:
   128→        if status != "fixed" or issue.get("detector") != "stale_wontfix":
   129→            return
   130→        detail = issue.get("detail", {})
   131→        if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   132→            return
   133→        original_issue_id = detail.get("original_issue_id")
   134→        if not isinstance(original_issue_id, str) or not original_issue_id:
   135→            return
   136→        original = state["work_items"].get(original_issue_id)
   137→        if not isinstance(original, dict) or original.get("status") != "wontfix":
   138→            return
   139→        snapshot_scan_count = int(state.get("scan_count", 0) or 0)
   140→        original["wontfix_scan_count"] = snapshot_scan_count
   141→        original["wontfix_snapshot"] = {
   142→            "captured_at": now,
   143→            "scan_count": snapshot_scan_count,
   144→            "tier": original.get("tier"),
   145→            "confidence": original.get("confidence"),
   146→            "detail": copy.deepcopy(original.get("detail", {})),
   147→        }
   148→
   149→    status_filter = "all" if status == "open" else "open"
   150→    for issue in match_issues(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter):
```

   103→def _make_tool_detect_command(
   104→    label: str,
   105→    runner: ToolResultRunner,
   106→) -> DetectCommand:
   107→    def command(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   108→        result = runner(Path(args.path))
   109→        if result.status == "error":
   110→            payload = {
   111→                "count": 0,
   112→                "entries": [],
   113→                "status": result.status,
   114→                "error_kind": result.error_kind,
   115→                "message": result.message,
   116→            }
   117→            if getattr(args, "json", False):
   118→                print(json.dumps(payload, indent=2))
   119→                return
   120→            print(colorize(f"\n{label} unavailable", "yellow"))
   121→            if result.message:
   122→                print(colorize(result.message, "dim"))
   123→            return
   124→
   125→        entries = [
   126→            {
   127→                "file": rel(entry["file"]),
   128→                "line": entry["line"],
   129→                "message": entry["message"],
   130→            }
   131→            for entry in result.entries
   132→        ]
   133→        display_entries(
   134→            args,
   135→            entries,
   136→            label=label,
   137→            empty_msg=f"No {label} findings.",
   138→            columns=["File", "Line", "Message"],
   139→            widths=[60, 6, 80],
   140→            row_fn=lambda entry: [
   141→                entry["file"],
   142→                str(entry["line"]),
   143→                entry["message"],
   144→            ],
   145→            json_payload={"count": len(entries), "entries": entries},
   146→        )
   147→
   148→    command.__module__ = __name__
   149→    return command
   150→def _make_entry_detect_command(
   151→    label: str,
   152→    detector_fn: EntryDetector,
   153→) -> DetectCommand:
   154→    def command(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   155→        entries, _ = detector_fn(Path(args.path))
   156→        display_entries(
   157→            args,
   158→            entries,
   159→            label=label,
   160→            empty_msg=f"No {label} findings.",
   161→            columns=["File", "Line", "Message"],
   162→            widths=[60, 6, 80],
   163→            row_fn=lambda entry: [
   164→                entry["file"],
   165→                str(entry["line"]),
   166→                entry["summary"],
   167→            ],
   168→            json_payload={"count": len(entries), "entries": entries},
   169→        )
   170→
   171→    command.__module__ = __name__
   172→    return command
   173→

1→"""Rust API-shape and public-surface policy detectors."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import resolve_path
     9→from desloppify.languages.rust.support import (
    10→    describe_rust_file,
    11→    find_rust_files,
    12→    has_public_api_markers,
    13→    read_text_or_none,
    14→    strip_rust_comments,
    15→)
    16→
    17→from ._shared import (
    18→    _argument_count,
    19→    _GETTER_RE,
    20→    _INTO_RE,
    21→    _NON_EXHAUSTIVE_RE,
    22→    _PUBLIC_ERROR_RE,
    23→    _PUBLIC_FIELD_RE,
    24→    _USE_STATEMENT_RE,
    25→    _WRAPPER_GETTER_NAMES,
    26→    _ENUM_VARIANT_RE,
    27→    _entry,
    28→    _has_manual_thread_contract,
    29→    _has_public_panic_path,
    30→    _has_python_binding_attrs,
    31→    _has_thread_assertion,
    32→    _is_internal_module,
    33→    _is_library_api_file,
    34→    _is_test_content,
    35→    _iter_public_functions,
    36→    _iter_public_types,
    37→    _looks_like_ffi_surface,
    38→    _looks_like_plain_getter,
    39→    _line_number,
    40→    _should_skip_future_proofing,
    41→    _starts_with_same_crate_import,
    42→    _group_files_by_manifest,
    43→)
    44→
    45→
    46→def detect_import_hygiene(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    47→    """Flag same-crate imports that should use `crate::...`."""
    48→    entries: list[dict] = []
    49→    files = find_rust_files(path)
    50→    for filepath in files:
    51→        absolute = Path(resolve_path(filepath))
    52→        content = read_text_or_none(absolute)
    53→        if content is None:
    54→            continue
    55→
    56→        context = describe_rust_file(absolute)
    57→        crate_name = context.crate_name
    58→        if not crate_name or not _is_internal_module(context):
    59→            continue
    60→
    61→        stripped = strip_rust_comments(content, preserve_lines=True)
    62→        for match in _USE_STATEMENT_RE.finditer(stripped):
    63→            statement = match.group(1).strip()
    64→            if not _starts_with_same_crate_import(statement, crate_name):
    65→                continue
    66→            line = _line_number(stripped, match.start())
    67→            entries.append(
    68→                _entry(
    69→                    absolute,
    70→                    line=line,
    71→                    name=f"crate_import::{line}",
    72→                    summary=f"Use `crate::` for same-crate imports instead of `{crate_name}::`",
    73→                    tier=2,
    74→                    confidence="high",
    75→                    detail=dict(crate_name=crate_name, statement=statement),
    76→                )
    77→            )
    78→    return entries, len(files)
    79→
    80→
    81→def detect_public_api_conventions(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    82→    """Flag high-confidence public API naming mismatches."""
    83→    entries: list[dict] = []
    84→    files = find_rust_files(path)
    85→    for filepath in files:
    86→        absolute = Path(resolve_path(filepath))
    87→        content = read_text_or_none(absolute)
    88→        if content is None:
    89→            continue
    90→        context = describe_rust_file(absolute)
    91→        if not _is_library_api_file(context) or not has_public_api_markers(content):
    92→            continue
    93→
    94→        for block in _iter_public_functions(content):
    95→            if _has_python_binding_attrs(block.attrs):
    96→                continue
    97→            if block.name in _WRAPPER_GETTER_NAMES:
    98→                continue
    99→            if _GETTER_RE.match(block.name) and _looks_like_plain_getter(block):
   100→                entries.append(
   101→                    _entry(
   102→                        absolute,
   103→                        line=block.line,
   104→                        name=f"getter::{block.name}",
   105→                        summary=(
   106→                            f"Public getter `{block.name}` uses a `get_` prefix; idiomatic Rust getters are usually bare names"
   107→                        ),
   108→                        tier=3,
   109→                        confidence="medium",
   110→                    )
   111→                )
   112→            elif (
   113→                _INTO_RE.match(block.name)
   114→                and block.receiver in {"&self", "&mut self"}
   115→                and _argument_count(block.signature) == 1
   116→            ):
   117→                entries.append(
   118→                    _entry(
   119→                        absolute,
   120→                        line=block.line,
   121→                        name=f"into_ref::{block.name}",
   122→                        summary=(
   123→                            f"Public method `{block.name}` is named `into_*` but borrows `self`; `into_*` is usually by-value in Rust"
   124→                        ),
   125→                        tier=3,
   126→                        confidence="medium",
   127→                    )
   128→                )
   129→    return entries, len(files)
   130→
   131→
   132→def detect_error_boundaries(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   133→    """Flag public API error boundaries that lean on app-style error handling."""
   134→    entries: list[dict] = []
   135→    files = find_rust_files(path)
   136→    for filepath in files:
   137→        absolute = Path(resolve_path(filepath))
   138→        content = read_text_or_none(absolute)
   139→        if content is None:
   140→            continue
   141→        context = describe_rust_file(absolute)
   142→        if not _is_library_api_file(context):
   143→            continue
   144→
   145→        for block in _iter_public_functions(content):
   146→            if _PUBLIC_ERROR_RE.search(block.signature):
   147→                entries.append(
   148→                    _entry(
   149→                        absolute,
   150→                        line=block.line,
   151→                        name=f"error_type::{block.name}",
   152→                        summary=(
   153→                            f"Public function `{block.name}` exposes an app-style error boundary; prefer a crate-specific error type on public APIs"
   154→                        ),
   155→                        tier=2,
   156→                        confidence="medium",
   157→                    )
   158→                )
   159→            if block.body and _has_public_panic_path(block.body):
   160→                entries.append(
   161→                    _entry(
   162→                        absolute,
   163→                        line=block.line,
   164→                        name=f"panic_path::{block.name}",
   165→                        summary=(
   166→                            f"Public function `{block.name}` contains `unwrap`/`expect`/panic-style control flow on a public path"
   167→                        ),
   168→                        tier=2,
   169→                        confidence="medium",
   170→                    )
   171→                )
   172→    return entries, len(files)
   173→
   174→
   175→def detect_future_proofing(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   176→    """Flag brittle public structs/enums that may want `#[non_exhaustive]`."""
   177→    entries: list[dict] = []
   178→    files = find_rust_files(path)
   179→    for filepath in files:
   180→        absolute = Path(resolve_path(filepath))
   181→        content = read_text_or_none(absolute)
   182→        if content is None:
   183→            continue
   184→        context = describe_rust_file(absolute)
   185→        if not _is_library_api_file(context):
   186→            continue
   187→
   188→        for block in _iter_public_types(content):
   189→            if _NON_EXHAUSTIVE_RE.search(block.attrs):
   190→                continue
   191→            if _should_skip_future_proofing(content, block):
   192→                continue
   193→            if block.kind == "struct":
   194→                public_fields = len(_PUBLIC_FIELD_RE.findall(block.body))
   195→                if public_fields >= 2:
   196→                    entries.append(
   197→                        _entry(
   198→                            absolute,
   199→                            line=block.line,
   200→                            name=f"struct::{block.name}",
```

   546→        """
   547→use std::sync::Mutex;
   548→use tokio::sync::RwLock;
   549→
   550→async fn hold_std_guard(state: &Mutex<String>) {
   551→    let guard = state.lock().unwrap();
   552→    consume().await;
   553→    drop(guard);
   554→}
   555→
   556→async fn hold_async_guard(state: &RwLock<String>) {
   557→    let guard = state.read().await;
   558→    consume().await;
   559→    drop(guard);
   560→}
   561→""",
   562→    )

   565→        entries, _ = detect_async_locking(tmp_path)
   566→

   568→        "std_guard::hold_std_guard",
   569→        "async_guard::hold_async_guard",
   570→    }

   752→        """
   753→use std::mem;
   754→
   755→fn decode(bytes: &[u8], index: usize, ptr: *const u8, len: usize) {
   756→    let _ = unsafe { mem::transmute::<*const u8, usize>(ptr) };
   757→    let _ = unsafe { bytes.get_unchecked(index) };
   758→    let _ = unsafe { std::slice::from_raw_parts(ptr, len) };
   759→    let _ = unsafe { std::str::from_utf8_unchecked(bytes) };
   760→    let _ = Some(index).unwrap_unchecked();
   761→}
   762→""",
   763→    )

   767→

   769→        "transmute",
   770→        "get_unchecked",
   771→        "from_raw_parts",
   772→        "from_utf8_unchecked",
   773→        "unwrap_unchecked",
   774→    }

  1003→def test_detect_unsafe_api_usage_ignores_documented_vec_rebuilds(tmp_path):
  1004→    _write(
  1005→        tmp_path,
  1006→        "Cargo.toml",
  1007→        '[package]\nname = "demo-app"\nversion = "0.1.0"\nedition = "2021"\n',
  1008→    )
  1009→    _write(
  1010→        tmp_path,
  1011→        "src/lib.rs",
  1012→        """
  1013→fn rebuild(ptr: *mut u8, len: usize, cap: usize) -> Vec<u8> {
  1014→    // The ptr + len always represents the end of that buffer.
  1015→    // Thus, we can safely reconstruct a Vec from it without leaking memory.
  1016→    let _unused = len;
  1017→    unsafe { Vec::from_raw_parts(ptr, len, cap) }
  1018→}
  1019→""",
  1020→    )
  1021→
  1022→    with runtime_scope(RuntimeContext(project_root=tmp_path)):

    77→            bypass=bool(getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)),
    78→            attest=getattr(args, "attest", "") or "",
    79→        )
    80→
    81→    return state_file, state, plan_access

    65→
    66→def triage_phase_banner(
    67→    plan: PlanModel,
    68→    state: dict | None = None,
    69→    *,
    70→    snapshot: TriageSnapshot | None = None,
    71→) -> str:
    72→    """Return a banner string describing triage status."""
    73→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    74→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
    75→    run_hint = (
    76→        f"Run: {TRIAGE_CMD_RUN_STAGES_CODEX} "
    77→        f"(or {TRIAGE_CMD_RUN_STAGES_CLAUDE})"
    78→    )
    79→    resolved_state = state or {}
    80→    resolved_snapshot = snapshot or build_triage_snapshot(plan, resolved_state)
    81→
    82→    if not resolved_snapshot.has_triage_in_queue:
    83→        undispositioned = len(resolved_snapshot.undispositioned_ids)
    84→        if undispositioned:
    85→            return (
    86→                "TRIAGE RECOVERY NEEDED — "
    87→                f"{undispositioned} review work item(s) still need cluster/skip dispositions. "
    88→                f"{run_hint}"
    89→            )
    90→        if resolved_snapshot.is_triage_stale or meta.get("triage_recommended"):
    91→            return (
    92→                "TRIAGE RECOMMENDED — review work items changed since last triage. "
    93→                f"{run_hint}"
    94→            )
    95→        return ""
    96→
    97→    if resolved_state and has_objective_backlog(resolved_state, None):
    98→        undispositioned = len(resolved_snapshot.undispositioned_ids)
    99→        if undispositioned:
   100→            return (
   101→                "TRIAGE PENDING — "
   102→                f"{undispositioned} review work item(s) still need cluster/skip dispositions after current work. "
   103→                f"{run_hint}"
   104→            )
   105→        return (
   106→            "TRIAGE PENDING — queued and will activate after objective work "
   107→            "is complete."
   108→        )
   109→    progress = resolved_snapshot.progress
   110→    if progress.completed_count:
   111→        total_stages = len(TRIAGE_STAGE_LABELS)
   112→        return (
   113→            f"TRIAGE MODE ({progress.completed_count}/{total_stages} stages recorded) — "
   114→            f"complete all stages to exit. {run_hint}"
   115→        )
   116→    return (
   117→        "TRIAGE MODE — review work items need analysis before fixing. "
   118→        f"{run_hint}"
   119→    )
   120→
   121→__all__ = [
   122→    "TRIAGE_CMD_CLUSTER_ADD",
   123→    "TRIAGE_CMD_CLUSTER_CREATE",
   124→    "TRIAGE_CMD_CLUSTER_ENRICH",
   125→    "TRIAGE_CMD_CLUSTER_ENRICH_COMPACT",

    43→class ShowOptions:

    55→        return cls(

    57→            show_code=bool(getattr(args, "code", False)),
    58→            chronic=bool(getattr(args, "chronic", False)),

    60→            output_file=getattr(args, "output", None),

    65→def _handle_special_entity_views(
    66→    *,
    67→    entity,
    68→    state: dict,
    69→    config: dict,
    70→    lang_name: str | None,
    71→    pattern_raw: str,
    72→) -> bool:
    73→    if entity.kind == "special_view" and entity.pattern.strip().lower() == "concerns":
    74→        _show_concerns(state, lang_name)
    75→        return True
    76→    if entity.kind == "dimension" and entity.is_subjective:
    77→        _render_subjective_dimension(state, config, entity, pattern_raw)
    78→        return True
    79→    return False
    80→
    81→
    82→def _load_entity_matches(
    83→    *,
    84→    state: dict,
    85→    entity,
    86→    pattern: str,
    87→    status_filter: str,
    88→    scope: str | None,
    89→    chronic: bool,
    90→) -> tuple[str, list[dict]] | None:
    91→    if entity.kind != "dimension" or entity.is_subjective:
    92→        return pattern, load_matches(
    93→            state, scope=scope, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=chronic
    94→        )
    95→    matches = _load_dimension_issues(state, entity, status_filter)
    96→    if not matches:
    97→        _render_clean_mechanical_dimension(state, entity)
    98→        return None
    99→    return entity.display_name, matches

    21→class ResolvedEntity:
    22→    """Classifies what the user's show pattern refers to."""
    23→

    31→def _resolve_special_view(pattern: str, lowered: str) -> ResolvedEntity | None:

    33→        return None
    34→    return ResolvedEntity(

    41→def _resolve_mechanical_dimension(pattern: str, lowered: str) -> ResolvedEntity | None:
    42→    lookup = _build_dimension_lookup()
    43→    detectors = lookup.get(lowered) or lookup.get(pattern.lower())
    44→    if not detectors:
    45→        return None
    46→    dim_display = pattern
    47→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
    48→        dim_lowered = dim.name.lower().replace(" ", "_")
    49→        if dim.name.lower() == lowered or dim_lowered == lowered:

    52→    return ResolvedEntity(

    54→        pattern=pattern,
    55→        display_name=dim_display,
    56→        detectors=tuple(detectors),

    66→    display_name = DISPLAY_NAMES.get(lowered)

    69→            if key.lower().replace(" ", "_") == lowered:

    72→    if not display_name:
    73→        return None
    74→    dim_data, display_name = _lookup_dimension_score(state, display_name)
    75→    is_subj = "subjective_assessment" in (
    76→        dim_data.get("detectors", {}) if isinstance(dim_data, dict) else {}
    77→    )
    78→    return ResolvedEntity(

    80→        pattern=pattern,
    81→        display_name=display_name,
    82→        detectors=(),

    86→
    87→def resolve_entity(pattern: str, state: StateModel) -> ResolvedEntity:
    88→    """Classify a user pattern as a dimension, special view, or passthrough.
    89→
    90→    Resolution priority:
    91→    1. Special views: "concerns", "subjective", "subjective_review"
    92→    2. Mechanical dimension name (from DIMENSIONS)
    93→    3. Subjective dimension name (from DISPLAY_NAMES / dimension_scores)
    94→    4. Everything else: file_or_pattern passthrough
    95→    """
    96→    lowered = pattern.strip().lower().replace(" ", "_")
    97→
    98→    # 1. Special views
    99→    special_view = _resolve_special_view(pattern, lowered)
   100→    if special_view is not None:
   101→        return special_view
   102→
   103→    # 2. Mechanical dimension (via DIMENSIONS list)

   109→    subjective = _resolve_subjective_dimension(pattern, lowered, state)
   110→    if subjective is not None:

   112→
   113→    # 4. Everything else
   114→    return ResolvedEntity(
   115→        kind="file_or_pattern",
   116→        pattern=pattern,
   117→        display_name=pattern,
   118→    )

   121→def _build_dimension_lookup() -> dict[str, list[str]]:
   122→    """Build a map from dimension name/key (lowered) to detector names."""
   123→    lookup: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   124→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:

   126→        lookup[dim.name.lower()] = detectors
   127→        # Also index by underscore key: "file_health" -> detectors

   130→            lookup[key] = detectors

   139→                detectors = list(dim.detectors) if hasattr(dim, "detectors") else []
   140→                if normalized_key not in lookup:
   141→                    lookup[normalized_key] = detectors
   142→                if normalized_display not in lookup:
   143→                    lookup[normalized_display] = detectors
   144→    return lookup

    41→def _validate_fixed_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> bool:

    44→    note = getattr(args, "note", None)
    45→    if validate_note_length(note):

    51→def _load_state_with_guards(
    52→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    53→    *,
    54→    attestation: str | None,
    55→) -> tuple[str, dict, object] | None:
    56→    """Validate inputs, apply guardrails, and load state for resolve."""
    57→    _validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)
    58→    if not _validate_fixed_note(args):
    59→        return None
    60→
    61→    state_file = state_path(args)
    62→    state = load_state(state_file)
    63→    plan_access = load_resolve_plan_access()
    64→
    65→    if not getattr(args, "force_resolve", False):
    66→        if _check_queue_order_guard(
    67→            state,
    68→            args.patterns,
    69→            args.status,
    70→            plan_access=plan_access,
    71→        ):
    72→            return None
    73→
    74→    if args.status == "fixed":
    75→        require_triage_current_or_exit(
    76→            state=state,
    77→            bypass=bool(getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)),
    78→            attest=getattr(args, "attest", "") or "",
    79→        )
    80→
    81→    return state_file, state, plan_access

    83→

    94→        args,

    96→        resolve_all_patterns_fn=_resolve_all_patterns,

    99→    prev = _previous_score_snapshot(state)

   102→        for dim, payload in (state.get("subjective_assessments") or {}).items()
   103→        if isinstance(dim, str)
   104→    }
   105→    all_resolved = _resolve_all_patterns(
   106→        state,
   107→        args,
   108→        attestation=attestation,
   109→        plan_access=plan_access,

   111→    return prev, prev_subjective_scores, all_resolved

   113→
   114→def cmd_resolve(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   115→    """Resolve issue(s) matching one or more patterns."""
   116→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   117→    loaded = _load_state_with_guards(args, attestation=attestation)
   118→    if loaded is None:
   119→        return
   120→    state_file, state, plan_access = loaded

   168→    if names:

   173→def resolve_show_scope(args: object) -> tuple[bool, str | None, str, str | None]:
   174→    """Resolve scope/pattern/status for a show invocation."""
   175→    chronic = getattr(args, "chronic", False)
   176→    pattern = args.pattern
   177→    status_filter = "open" if chronic else getattr(args, "status", "open")
   178→    if chronic:
   179→        scope = pattern
   180→        pattern = pattern or "<chronic>"
   181→        return True, pattern, status_filter, scope
   182→    if not pattern:
   183→        print(
   184→            colorize(
   185→                "Pattern required (or use --chronic). Try: desloppify show --help",
   186→                "yellow",
   187→            )
   188→        )
   189→        return False, None, status_filter, ""
   190→    return True, pattern, status_filter, pattern
   191→
   192→
   193→def load_matches(
   194→    state: StateModel,
   195→    *,
   196→    scope: str | None,
   197→    status_filter: str,
   198→    chronic: bool,
   199→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   200→    """Load matching issues from the ranked queue."""
   201→    queue = build_work_queue(
   202→        state,
   203→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
   204→            count=None,
   205→            scope=scope,
   206→            status=status_filter,
   207→            include_subjective=False,
   208→            chronic=chronic,
   209→        ),
   210→    )
   211→    return [item for item in queue.get("items", []) if item.get("kind") == "issue"]
   212→
   213→
   214→def resolve_noise(
   215→    config: dict[str, Any],
   216→    matches: list[dict[str, Any]],
   217→    *,
   218→    no_budget: bool = False,
   219→) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, int], int, int, str | None]:
   220→    """Apply detector/global noise budget to show matches.
   221→
   222→    When *no_budget* is True, all matches are surfaced (nothing hidden).
   223→    """
   224→    if no_budget:
   225→        return (
   226→            matches,
   227→            {},
   228→            0,
   229→            0,
   230→            None,
   231→        )
   232→    noise_budget, global_noise_budget, budget_warning = (
   233→        state_mod.resolve_issue_noise_settings(config)
   234→    )
   235→    surfaced_matches, hidden_by_detector = state_mod.apply_issue_noise_budget(
   236→        matches,
   237→        budget=noise_budget,
   238→        global_budget=global_noise_budget,
   239→    )
   240→    return (
   241→        surfaced_matches,
   242→        hidden_by_detector,
   243→        noise_budget,
   244→        global_noise_budget,
   245→        budget_warning,
   246→    )
   247→

     6→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.finalize import finalize_queue

    19→def build_work_queue(
    20→    state: StateModel,
    21→    *,
    22→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
    23→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    24→    """Build the active canonical work queue for the current invocation."""
    25→    return _build_work_queue_with_visibility(
    26→        state,
    27→        options=options,
    28→        visibility=QueueVisibility.ALL,
    29→    )
    30→
    31→
    32→def _build_work_queue_with_visibility(
    33→    state: StateModel,
    34→    *,
    35→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
    36→    visibility: str = QueueVisibility.ALL,
    37→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    38→    """Build a ranked work queue from canonical queue partitions.
    39→
    40→    Open-status queue views are resolved from the canonical queue snapshot so
    41→    lifecycle phase, execution visibility, and backlog visibility all come
    42→    from one source of truth. Non-open status views remain issue-list queries.
    43→    """
    44→    opts = options or QueueBuildOptions()
    45→    plan, scan_path, status, threshold = resolve_queue_inputs(opts, state)
    46→    items = select_queue_items(
    47→        state,
    48→        opts=opts,
    49→        plan=plan,
    50→        scan_path=scan_path,
    51→        status=status,
    52→        threshold=threshold,
    53→        visibility=visibility,
    54→    )
    55→    return finalize_queue(items, state=state, plan=plan, opts=opts)
    56→

    59→    "QueueBuildOptions",

1→"""Queue item selection from canonical snapshot or raw issue lists."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import scope_matches
     6→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.inputs import gather_subjective_items
     7→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.models import QueueBuildOptions, QueueVisibility
     8→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.ranking import build_issue_items
     9→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.snapshot import build_queue_snapshot
    10→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.types import WorkQueueItem
    11→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    12→
    13→
    14→def select_queue_items(
    15→    state: StateModel,
    16→    *,
    17→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
    18→    plan: dict | None,
    19→    scan_path: str | None,
    20→    status: str,
    21→    threshold: float,
    22→    visibility: str,
    23→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
    24→    """Select the raw queue item list before ranking/finalization."""
    25→    if status == "open" and not opts.chronic:
    26→        snapshot = build_queue_snapshot(
    27→            state,
    28→            options=opts,
    29→            plan=plan,
    30→            target_strict=threshold,
    31→        )
    32→        return filter_snapshot_items(
    33→            items_for_visibility(snapshot=snapshot, visibility=visibility),
    34→            opts,
    35→        )
    36→
    37→    items = build_issue_items(
    38→        state,
    39→        scan_path=scan_path,
    40→        status_filter=status,
    41→        scope=opts.scope,
    42→        chronic=opts.chronic,
    43→    )
    44→    if opts.include_subjective and status == "all":
    45→        items += gather_subjective_items(state, opts, threshold)
    46→    return items
    47→
    48→
    49→def items_for_visibility(*, snapshot, visibility: str) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
    50→    """Select the snapshot partition for one queue surface."""
    51→    if visibility == QueueVisibility.BACKLOG:
    52→        source_items = snapshot.backlog_items or snapshot.execution_items
    53→        return [
    54→            dict(item)
    55→            for item in source_items
    56→            if item.get("kind") not in {"workflow_stage", "workflow_action"}
    57→        ]
    58→    return [dict(item) for item in snapshot.execution_items]
    59→
    60→
    61→def filter_snapshot_items(
    62→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
    63→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
    64→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
    65→    """Apply view-local filtering after snapshot partition selection."""
    66→    filtered = items
    67→    if not opts.include_subjective:
    68→        filtered = [
    69→            item for item in filtered
    70→            if item.get("kind") != "subjective_dimension"
    71→        ]
    72→    if opts.scope:
    73→        filtered = [
    74→            item for item in filtered
    75→            if item.get("kind") != "subjective_dimension" or scope_matches(item, opts.scope)
    76→        ]
    77→    return filtered
    78→
    79→
    80→__all__ = [
    81→    "filter_snapshot_items",
    82→    "items_for_visibility",
    83→    "select_queue_items",
    84→]
    85→

1→"""Shared degraded-plan warning helpers for resolve flows."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import sys
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     9→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.context import resolve_plan_load_status
    10→
    11→
    12→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    13→class DegradedPlanWarning:
    14→    """Structured degraded-mode warning payload for resolve flows."""
    15→
    16→    command_label: str
    17→    error_kind: str | None
    18→    message: str
    19→    behavior: str
    20→
    21→
    22→@dataclass
    23→class DegradedPlanWarningState:
    24→    """Mutable per-call-chain dedupe state for degraded resolve warnings."""
    25→
    26→    warned: bool = False
    27→
    28→
    29→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    30→class ResolvePlanAccess:
    31→    """Resolved living-plan access contract for one resolve attempt."""
    32→
    33→    plan: dict | None
    34→    degraded: bool
    35→    error_kind: str | None
    36→    warning_state: DegradedPlanWarningState
    37→    recovery: str | None = None
    38→
    39→    def usable_plan(self, *, behavior: str, command_label: str = "resolve") -> dict | None:
    40→        """Return the loaded plan, warning once when resolve falls back."""
    41→        if self.degraded:
    42→            warn_plan_load_degraded_once(
    43→                command_label=command_label,
    44→                error_kind=self.error_kind,
    45→                behavior=behavior,
    46→                warning_state=self.warning_state,
    47→            )
    48→            if self.recovery == "backup":
    49→                return self.plan if isinstance(self.plan, dict) else None
    50→            return None
    51→        return self.plan if isinstance(self.plan, dict) else None
    52→
    53→
    54→def load_resolve_plan_access(
    55→    *,
    56→    warning_state: DegradedPlanWarningState | None = None,
    57→) -> ResolvePlanAccess:
    58→    """Resolve plan access once so all degraded behavior shares one warning state."""
    59→    resolved_warning_state = warning_state or DegradedPlanWarningState()
    60→    status = resolve_plan_load_status()
    61→    return ResolvePlanAccess(
    62→        plan=status.plan,
    63→        degraded=status.degraded,
    64→        error_kind=status.error_kind,
    65→        recovery=getattr(status, "recovery", None),
    66→        warning_state=resolved_warning_state,
    67→    )
    68→
    69→
    70→def warn_plan_load_degraded_once(
    71→    *,
    72→    command_label: str = "resolve",
    73→    error_kind: str | None,
    74→    behavior: str,
    75→    warning_state: DegradedPlanWarningState | None = None,
    76→) -> DegradedPlanWarning | None:
    77→    """Print one consistent warning when resolve behavior degrades.
    78→
    79→    Returns a structured warning payload on first emission, else ``None``.
    80→    Dedupe is scoped to the provided ``warning_state``; unrelated resolve
    81→    attempts should pass separate state objects and will warn independently.
    82→    """
    83→    if warning_state is not None:
    84→        if warning_state.warned:
    85→            return None
    86→        warning_state.warned = True
    87→
    88→    detail = f" ({error_kind})" if error_kind else ""
    89→    message = (
    90→        f"Warning: {command_label} is running in degraded mode because the living "
    91→        f"plan could not be loaded{detail}."
    92→    )
    93→    warning = DegradedPlanWarning(
    94→        command_label=command_label,
    95→        error_kind=error_kind,
    96→        message=message,
    97→        behavior=behavior,
    98→    )
    99→    print(
   100→        colorize(f"  {warning.message}", "yellow"),
   101→        file=sys.stderr,
   102→    )
   103→    print(
   104→        colorize(f"  {warning.behavior}", "dim"),
   105→        file=sys.stderr,
   106→    )
   107→    return warning
   108→
   109→
   110→def _reset_degraded_plan_warning_for_tests() -> None:
   111→    """Backward-compatible no-op kept for existing tests."""
   112→    return None
   113→
   114→
   115→__all__ = [
   116→    "DegradedPlanWarning",
   117→    "DegradedPlanWarningState",
   118→    "ResolvePlanAccess",
   119→    "load_resolve_plan_access",
   120→    "warn_plan_load_degraded_once",
   121→]
   122→
