```
1→"""Ranking and grouping helpers for work queue selection."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→from typing import Any, cast
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.registry import DETECTORS
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.constants import TRIAGE_STAGE_ORDER
    10→from desloppify.engine._scoring.results.health import compute_health_breakdown
    11→from desloppify.engine._scoring.results.impact import get_dimension_for_detector
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import path_scoped_issues
    13→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    14→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import (
    15→    ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY,
    16→    detail_dict,
    17→    is_review_issue,
    18→    is_subjective_issue,
    19→    primary_command_for_issue,
    20→    review_issue_weight,
    21→    scope_matches,
    22→    slugify,
    23→    status_matches,
    24→    supported_fixers_for_item,
    25→    workflow_stage_name,
    26→)
    27→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.ranking_output import (
    28→    group_queue_items,
    29→    item_explain,
    30→    subjective_score_value,
    31→)
    32→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.synthetic import subjective_strict_scores
    33→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.types import WorkQueueItem
    34→from desloppify.engine.planning.helpers import CONFIDENCE_ORDER
    35→
    36→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    37→
    38→# Plan-aware sort tiers (item_sort_key)
    39→_TIER_PLANNED = 0   # Items with explicit plan position
    40→_TIER_EXISTING = 1  # Known items, natural ranking
    41→_TIER_NEW = 2       # Newly discovered items
    42→
    43→# Natural ranking groups (_natural_sort_key)
    44→_RANK_INITIAL_REVIEW = -3  # Unassessed subjective dimensions
    45→_RANK_WORKFLOW = -2         # Score checkpoints, create-plan
    46→_RANK_TRIAGE_STAGE = -1     # Epic triage workflow stages
    47→_RANK_CLUSTER = 0           # Auto-clustered issues
    48→_RANK_ISSUE = 1           # Individual issues + assessed subjective
    49→
    50→def _workflow_stage_index(item: WorkQueueItem) -> int:
    51→    raw_index = item.get("stage_index")
    52→    if raw_index is not None:
    53→        if isinstance(raw_index, int):
    54→            return raw_index
    55→        if isinstance(raw_index, str):
    56→            stripped = raw_index.strip()
    57→            if stripped.lstrip("-").isdigit():
    58→                return int(stripped)
    59→        logger.warning("Invalid workflow stage index %r", raw_index)
    60→    return TRIAGE_STAGE_ORDER.get(workflow_stage_name(item).lower(), 0)
    61→
    62→
    63→def enrich_with_impact(
    64→    items: list[WorkQueueItem], dimension_scores: dict[str, Any]
    65→) -> None:
    66→    """Stamp ``estimated_impact`` on each item based on dimension-level headroom.
    67→
    68→    Impact = ``overall_per_point * headroom`` where headroom = ``100 - score``.
    69→    Items in dimensions with more score headroom sort first.
    70→    """
    71→    if not dimension_scores:
    72→        for item in items:
    73→            item["estimated_impact"] = 0.0
    74→        return
    75→
    76→    breakdown = compute_health_breakdown(dimension_scores, score_key="strict")
    77→    entries = breakdown.get("entries", [])
    78→
    79→    # Build lookup: normalized dimension name -> {per_point, headroom}
    80→    dim_impact: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
    81→    for entry in entries:
    82→        name = str(entry.get("name", "")).strip()
    83→        if not name:
    84→            continue
    85→        per_point = float(entry.get("overall_per_point", 0.0))
    86→        score = float(entry.get("score", 0.0))
    87→        headroom = 100.0 - score
    88→        dim_impact[name.lower()] = {"per_point": per_point, "headroom": headroom}
    89→
    90→    for item in items:
    91→        impact = _compute_item_impact(item, dim_impact, get_dimension_for_detector)
    92→        item["estimated_impact"] = impact
    93→
    94→
    95→def _compute_item_impact(
    96→    item: WorkQueueItem,
    97→    dim_impact: dict[str, dict[str, float]],
    98→    get_dimension_for_detector,
    99→) -> float:
   100→    """Compute impact value for a single queue item."""
   101→    kind = item.get("kind", "issue")
   102→
   103→    # Subjective items (synthetic dimensions or subjective issues):
   104→    # look up by detail.dimension_name
   105→    if kind == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
   106→        dim_name = detail_dict(item).get("dimension_name", "")
   107→        entry = dim_impact.get(dim_name.lower())
   108→        if entry:
   109→            return entry["per_point"] * entry["headroom"]
   110→        return 0.0
   111→
   112→    # Mechanical issues: use detector -> dimension mapping
   113→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
   114→    if detector:
   115→        dimension = get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
   116→        if dimension:
   117→            entry = dim_impact.get(dimension.name.lower())
   118→            if entry:
   119→                return entry["per_point"] * entry["headroom"]
   120→
   121→    return 0.0
   122→
   123→
   124→def build_issue_items(
   125→    state: StateModel,
   126→    *,
   127→    scan_path: str | None,
   128→    status_filter: str,
   129→    scope: str | None,
   130→    chronic: bool,
   131→    forced_ids: set[str] | None = None,
   132→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   133→    scoped = path_scoped_issues((state.get("work_items") or state.get("issues", {})), scan_path)
   134→    subjective_scores = subjective_strict_scores(state)
   135→    out: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
   136→    forced_ids = forced_ids or set()
   137→
   138→    for issue_id, issue in scoped.items():
   139→        if issue.get("suppressed"):
   140→            continue
   141→        if not status_matches(issue.get("status", "open"), status_filter):
   142→            continue
   143→        if chronic and not (
   144→            issue.get("status") == "open" and issue.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2
   145→        ):
   146→            continue
   147→
   148→        # Evidence-only: skip issues below standalone confidence threshold
   149→        detector = issue.get("detector", "")
   150→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   151→        if issue_id not in forced_ids and meta and meta.standalone_threshold:
   152→            threshold_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(meta.standalone_threshold, 9)
   153→            issue_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(issue.get("confidence", "low"), 9)
   154→            if issue_rank > threshold_rank:
   155→                continue
   156→
   157→        item = cast(WorkQueueItem, dict(issue))
   158→        item["id"] = issue_id
   159→        item["kind"] = "issue"
   160→        item["action_type"] = meta.action_type if meta is not None else "manual_fix"
   161→        item["is_review"] = is_review_issue(item)
   162→        item["is_subjective"] = is_subjective_issue(item)
   163→        item["review_weight"] = (
   164→            review_issue_weight(item) if item["is_review"] else None
   165→        )
   166→        subjective_score = None
   167→        if item["is_subjective"]:
   168→            detail = detail_dict(issue)
   169→            dim_name = detail.get("dimension_name", "")
   170→            dim_key = detail.get("dimension", "") or slugify(dim_name)
   171→            subjective_score = subjective_scores.get(
   172→                dim_key, subjective_scores.get(dim_name.lower(), 100.0)
   173→            )
   174→        item["subjective_score"] = subjective_score
   175→        supported_fixers = supported_fixers_for_item(state, item)
   176→        item["primary_command"] = primary_command_for_issue(
   177→            item,
   178→            supported_fixers=supported_fixers,
   179→        )
   180→
   181→        if not scope_matches(item, scope):
   182→            continue
   183→        out.append(item)
   184→
   185→    return out
   186→
   187→
   188→def _natural_sort_key(item: WorkQueueItem) -> tuple:
   189→    """Compute natural (non-plan-aware) ranking for queue items."""
   190→    kind = item.get("kind", "issue")
   191→
   192→    # Initial-review subjective items: highest priority
   193→    if kind == "subjective_dimension" and item.get("initial_review"):
   194→        return (_RANK_INITIAL_REVIEW, 0, subjective_score_value(item), item.get("id", ""))
   195→
   196→    # Triage stage items: stage order, blocked after unblocked
   197→    if kind == "workflow_stage":
   198→        blocked_penalty = 1 if item.get("is_blocked") else 0
   199→        stage_index = _workflow_stage_index(item)
   200→        return (_RANK_TRIAGE_STAGE, blocked_penalty, stage_index, item.get("id", ""))
```

Perfect! Now I can see that "review" and "concerns" are non-objective detectors. The PR uses `_REVIEW_DETECTORS = frozenset({"review", "concerns"})` which aligns with this. Now let me look at the logic more carefully. Let me trace through the new logic in guardrails.py more carefully:

The PR shows:

1. **New field in TriageGuardrailResult**: `pending_behind_objective_backlog: bool = False`
2. **New detection logic in triage_guardrail_status**: checks if:
   - Triage is stale
   - Triage is NOT already in queue (`not snapshot.has_triage_in_queue`)
   - State exists (`bool(resolved_state)`)
   - Plan is mid-cycle (`is_mid_cycle(resolved_plan)`)
   - Objective backlog exists (`has_objective_backlog(resolved_state, None)`)

3. **New conditional in require_triage_current_or_exit**: 
   - If `pending_behind_objective_backlog` AND patterns are provided:
     - Get matched targets using `_matched_open_targets()`
     - If matched targets exist AND they're NOT review work:
       - Print banner with yellow
       - Return (allow the operation)
   - If pending but targets DO include review work:
     - Raise CommandError (block)

4. **Banner update in triage_phase_banner**: Add a new condition that returns a "TRIAGE PENDING" message when triage is stale but deferred.

```
1→"""Pruning helpers for auto-generated plan clusters."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→def prune_stale_clusters(
     7→    plan: dict,
     8→    issues: dict,
     9→    clusters: dict,
    10→    active_auto_keys: set[str],
    11→    now: str,
    12→) -> int:
    13→    """Delete auto-clusters that no longer have matching groups."""
    14→    changes = 0
    15→    for name in list(clusters.keys()):
    16→        cluster = clusters[name]
    17→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
    18→            continue
    19→        cluster_key = cluster.get("cluster_key", "")
    20→        if cluster_key in active_auto_keys:
    21→            continue
    22→        if cluster.get("user_modified"):
    23→            alive = [
    24→                issue_id
    25→                for issue_id in cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
    26→                if issue_id in issues and issues[issue_id].get("status") == "open"
    27→            ]
    28→            if alive:
    29→                if len(alive) != len(cluster.get("issue_ids", [])):
    30→                    cluster["issue_ids"] = alive
    31→                    cluster["updated_at"] = now
    32→                    changes += 1
    33→                continue
    34→        del clusters[name]
    35→        for issue_id in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
    36→            override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(issue_id)
    37→            if override and override.get("cluster") == name:
    38→                override["cluster"] = None
    39→                override["updated_at"] = now
    40→        if plan.get("active_cluster") == name:
    41→            plan["active_cluster"] = None
    42→        changes += 1
    43→    return changes
    44→
    45→
    46→__all__ = ["prune_stale_clusters"]
    47→
```

```
73→
    74→def verify_disappeared(
    75→    existing: dict,
    76→    current_ids: set[str],
    77→    suspect_detectors: set[str],
    78→    now: str,
    79→    *,
    80→    lang: str | None,
    81→    scan_path: str | None,
    82→    exclude: tuple[str, ...] = (),
    83→) -> tuple[int, int, int, set[str]]:
    84→    """Update scan corroboration for issues absent from scan.
    85→
    86→    Returns (resolved_count, skipped_other_lang, resolved_out_of_scope, changed_detectors).
    87→    Queue-tracked work stays user-controlled: disappearing from scan does not
    88→    change an open issue to resolved. Manually resolved items can be marked as
    89→    scan-verified when they remain absent.
    90→    """
    91→    resolved = skipped_other_lang = resolved_out_of_scope = 0
    92→    resolved_detectors: set[str] = set()
    93→
    94→    for issue_id, previous in existing.items():
    95→        previous_status = previous.get("status")
    96→        if issue_id in current_ids or previous_status not in (
    97→            "open",
    98→            "wontfix",
    99→            "fixed",
   100→            "false_positive",
   101→        ):
   102→            continue
   103→
   104→        if lang and previous.get("lang") and previous["lang"] != lang:
   105→            skipped_other_lang += 1
   106→            continue
   107→
   108→        # Suspect detectors (e.g. 'review') are import-only and must never
   109→        # be auto-resolved by a scan — check this BEFORE the scope filter
   110→        # so that review issues with file="." aren't wrongly resolved as
   111→        # "out of current scan scope".
   112→        if previous.get("detector", "unknown") in suspect_detectors:
   113→            continue
   114→
   115→        if scan_path and scan_path != ".":
   116→            prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
   117→            if (
   118→                not previous["file"].startswith(prefix)
   119→                and previous["file"] != scan_path
   120→            ):
   121→                if previous_status != "open":
   122→                    scope_note = f"Still absent in current scan scope ({scan_path})"
   123→                    _mark_scan_verified(
   124→                        previous,
   125→                        now,
   126→                        note=scope_note,
   127→                        attestation_text=scope_note,
   128→                    )
   129→                    resolved_detectors.add(previous.get("detector", "unknown"))
   130→                    resolved_out_of_scope += 1
   131→                continue
   132→
   133→        if exclude and any(matches_exclusion(previous["file"], ex) for ex in exclude):
   134→            continue
   135→
   136→        if previous_status == "open":
   137→            continue
   138→
   139→        verification_note = (
   140→            "Still absent from scan after manual wontfix"
   141→            if previous_status == "wontfix"
   142→            else "Still absent from scan after manual resolution"
   143→        )
   144→        _mark_scan_verified(
   145→            previous,
   146→            now,
   147→            note=verification_note,
   148→            attestation_text="Absent from detector output in latest scan",
   149→        )
   150→        resolved_detectors.add(previous.get("detector", "unknown"))
   151→        resolved += 1
   152→
   153→    return resolved, skipped_other_lang, resolved_out_of_scope, resolved_detectors
   154→
   155→
   156→def upsert_issues(
   157→    existing: dict,
   158→    current_issues: list[dict],
   159→    ignore: list[str],
   160→    now: str,
   161→    *,
   162→    lang: str | None,
   163→) -> tuple[set[str], int, int, dict[str, int], int, set[str]]:
   164→    """Insert new issues and update existing ones.
   165→
   166→    Returns (current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, by_detector, ignored_count, changed_detectors).
   167→    """
   168→    current_ids: set[str] = set()
   169→    new_count = reopened_count = ignored_count = 0
   170→    by_detector: dict[str, int] = {}
   171→    changed_detectors: set[str] = set()
```

```
1→"""Canonical triage coverage and status snapshot helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→
     7→from desloppify.engine._plan.constants import TRIAGE_IDS, is_synthetic_id
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.policy.stale import open_review_ids
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import Cluster, PlanModel
    10→from desloppify.engine._plan.triage.playbook import TriageProgress, compute_triage_progress
    11→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    12→
    13→
    14→def _normalized_issue_id_list(raw_ids: object) -> list[str]:
    15→    normalized: list[str] = []
    16→    seen: set[str] = set()
    17→    if not isinstance(raw_ids, list):
    18→        return normalized
    19→    for raw_id in raw_ids:
    20→        if not isinstance(raw_id, str):
    21→            continue
    22→        issue_id = raw_id.strip()
    23→        if not issue_id or issue_id in seen:
    24→            continue
    25→        seen.add(issue_id)
    26→        normalized.append(issue_id)
    27→    return normalized
    28→
    29→
    30→def _cluster_issue_ids(cluster: Cluster) -> list[str]:
    31→    ordered: list[str] = []
    32→    seen: set[str] = set()
    33→
    34→    def _append(raw_ids: object) -> None:
    35→        if not isinstance(raw_ids, list):
    36→            return
    37→        for raw_id in raw_ids:
    38→            if not isinstance(raw_id, str):
    39→                continue
    40→            issue_id = raw_id.strip()
    41→            if not issue_id or issue_id in seen:
    42→                continue
    43→            seen.add(issue_id)
    44→            ordered.append(issue_id)
    45→
    46→    _append(cluster.get("issue_ids"))
    47→    steps = cluster.get("action_steps")
    48→    if steps is not None:
    49→        for step in steps:
    50→            if not isinstance(step, dict):
    51→                continue
    52→            _append(step.get("issue_refs"))
    53→    return ordered
    54→
    55→
    56→def plan_review_ids(plan: PlanModel) -> list[str]:
    57→    """Return review/concerns IDs currently represented in queue_order."""
    58→    return [
    59→        issue_id
    60→        for issue_id in plan.get("queue_order", [])
    61→        if isinstance(issue_id, str)
    62→        and not is_synthetic_id(issue_id)
    63→        and (issue_id.startswith("review::") or issue_id.startswith("concerns::"))
    64→    ]
    65→
    66→
    67→def coverage_open_ids(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel) -> set[str]:
    68→    """Return the frozen or live open review IDs covered by this triage run."""
    69→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
    70→    active_ids = _normalized_issue_id_list(meta.get("active_triage_issue_ids"))
    71→    if active_ids:
    72→        return set(active_ids)
    73→    has_completed_scan = bool(state.get("last_scan"))
    74→    review_ids = open_review_ids(state)
    75→    if not has_completed_scan and not review_ids:
    76→        return set(plan_review_ids(plan))
    77→    return review_ids
    78→
    79→
    80→def active_triage_issue_ids(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel | None = None) -> set[str]:
    81→    """Return the frozen review issue set for the current triage run."""
    82→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
    83→    active_ids = _normalized_issue_id_list(meta.get("active_triage_issue_ids"))
    84→    if active_ids:
    85→        return set(active_ids)
    86→    if state is None:
    87→        return set()
    88→    return coverage_open_ids(plan, state)
    89→
    90→
    91→def _explicit_active_triage_issue_ids(plan: PlanModel) -> set[str]:
    92→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
    93→    return set(_normalized_issue_id_list(meta.get("active_triage_issue_ids")))
    94→
    95→
    96→def live_active_triage_issue_ids(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel | None = None) -> set[str]:
    97→    """Return frozen triage IDs that are still open review issues in state."""
    98→    frozen_ids = active_triage_issue_ids(plan, state)
    99→    if state is None or not frozen_ids:
   100→        return frozen_ids
   101→    return frozen_ids & open_review_ids(state)
   102→
   103→
   104→def undispositioned_triage_issue_ids(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel | None = None) -> list[str]:
   105→    """Return frozen triage issues still lacking cluster/skip/dismiss coverage."""
   106→    target_ids = live_active_triage_issue_ids(plan, state)
   107→    if not target_ids:
   108→        return []
   109→
   110→    covered_ids: set[str] = set()
   111→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   112→        if cluster.get("auto"):
   113→            continue
   114→        covered_ids.update(_cluster_issue_ids(cluster))
   115→
   116→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
   117→    covered_ids.update(issue_id for issue_id in skipped if isinstance(issue_id, str))
   118→
   119→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   120→    covered_ids.update(_normalized_issue_id_list(meta.get("dismissed_ids")))
   121→
   122→    dispositions = meta.get("issue_dispositions", {})
   123→    for issue_id, disposition in dispositions.items():
   124→        if disposition.get("decision_source") == "observe_auto" and issue_id in skipped:
   125→            covered_ids.add(issue_id)
   126→
   127→    return sorted(issue_id for issue_id in target_ids if issue_id not in covered_ids)
   128→
   129→
   130→def triage_coverage(
   131→    plan: PlanModel,
   132→    open_review_ids: set[str] | None = None,
   133→) -> tuple[int, int, dict[str, Cluster]]:
   134→    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for review issues in triage."""
   135→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   136→    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
   137→    for cluster in clusters.values():
   138→        all_cluster_ids.update(_cluster_issue_ids(cluster))
   139→    review_ids = list(open_review_ids) if open_review_ids is not None else plan_review_ids(plan)
   140→    organized = sum(1 for issue_id in review_ids if issue_id in all_cluster_ids)
   141→    return organized, len(review_ids), clusters
   142→
   143→
   144→def manual_clusters_with_issues(plan: PlanModel) -> list[str]:
   145→    """Return manual clusters that currently own at least one issue."""
   146→    return [
   147→        name
   148→        for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items()
   149→        if _cluster_issue_ids(cluster) and not cluster.get("auto")
   150→    ]
   151→
   152→
   153→def find_cluster_for(issue_id: str, clusters: dict[str, Cluster]) -> str | None:
   154→    """Return the owning cluster name for an issue ID, if any."""
   155→    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
   156→        if issue_id in _cluster_issue_ids(cluster):
   157→            return name
   158→    return None
   159→
   160→
   161→@dataclass(frozen=True)
   162→class TriageSnapshot:
   163→    """Single shared triage-status snapshot for command surfaces."""
   164→
   165→    progress: TriageProgress
   166→    frozen_issue_ids: frozenset[str]
   167→    live_open_ids: frozenset[str]
   168→    new_since_triage_ids: frozenset[str]
   169→    undispositioned_ids: frozenset[str]
   170→    organized_count: int
   171→    total_in_scope: int
   172→    has_triage_in_queue: bool
   173→    is_triage_stale: bool
   174→    cycle_active: bool
   175→    triage_has_run: bool
   176→
   177→
   178→def build_triage_snapshot(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel) -> TriageSnapshot:
   179→    """Build a canonical triage snapshot from plan and state."""
   180→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   181→    triaged_ids = set(_normalized_issue_id_list(meta.get("triaged_ids")))
   182→    frozen_ids = _explicit_active_triage_issue_ids(plan) & open_review_ids(state)
   183→    live_open = open_review_ids(state)
   184→    known_ids = triaged_ids | frozen_ids
   185→    new_since = live_open - known_ids if known_ids else set()
   186→    in_scope_ids = coverage_open_ids(plan, state)
   187→    organized_count, total_in_scope, _clusters = triage_coverage(
   188→        plan,
   189→        open_review_ids=in_scope_ids,
   190→    )
   191→
   192→    return TriageSnapshot(
   193→        progress=compute_triage_progress(meta.get("triage_stages", {})),
   194→        frozen_issue_ids=frozenset(frozen_ids),
   195→        live_open_ids=frozenset(live_open),
   196→        new_since_triage_ids=frozenset(new_since),
   197→        undispositioned_ids=frozenset(undispositioned_triage_issue_ids(plan, state)),
   198→        organized_count=organized_count,
   199→        total_in_scope=total_in_scope,
   200→        has_triage_in_queue=bool(set(plan.get("queue_order", [])) & TRIAGE_IDS),
   201→        is_triage_stale=bool(new_since),
   202→        cycle_active=bool(frozen_ids),
   203→        triage_has_run=bool(triaged_ids),
   204→    )
   205→
   206→
   207→__all__ = [
   208→    "TriageSnapshot",
   209→    "active_triage_issue_ids",
   210→    "build_triage_snapshot",
   211→    "coverage_open_ids",
   212→    "find_cluster_for",
   213→    "live_active_triage_issue_ids",
   214→    "manual_clusters_with_issues",
   215→    "plan_review_ids",
   216→    "triage_coverage",
   217→    "undispositioned_triage_issue_ids",
   218→]
   219→
```

Perfect. Now let me check if there are any issues with the logic. Let me check some edge cases:

1. **Edge case 1**: What if `patterns` is `None` in require_triage_current_or_exit?
   Looking at the code: `if result.pending_behind_objective_backlog and patterns:` - so it checks `and patterns`, which means if patterns is None or empty list, it skips this block and goes to the next check.

2. **Edge case 2**: What if patterns match nothing?
   `_matched_open_targets()` would return an empty list, and then `if matched_targets and ...` would be False, so it wouldn't return early.

3. **Edge case 3**: What if the pattern matches something but ALL matches are review work?
   Then `_targets_include_review_work()` returns True, condition fails, and it falls through to the error case.

Wait, I see a potential issue. Let me re-read the logic more carefully:

```python
if result.pending_behind_objective_backlog and patterns:
    matched_targets = _matched_open_targets(state, patterns)
    if matched_targets and not _targets_include_review_work(matched_targets):
        # Print banner and return (ALLOW)
        return
```

This says: if pending AND we have matching targets AND they're NOT review work, then allow it.

But then later:

```python
new_ids = result.new_ids
if result.pending_behind_objective_backlog:
    lines = [...]
    raise CommandError(...)
```

This means if we fall through (because patterns were empty, or matched_targets is empty, or they include review work), it raises an error.

So the flow is:
1. If pending AND patterns AND targets match AND NOT review → allow
2. Else if pending → error
3. Else → check existing logic

This seems correct! Let me verify with the test cases:

**Test 1**: `require_triage_current_allows_objective_resolve_while_pending`
- patterns = ["obj::1"] → matches obj::1 with detector="complexity" (NOT review)
- pending_behind_objective_backlog = TRUE
- matched_targets = [obj::1 issue]
- _targets_include_review_work([obj::1]) = FALSE
- So it prints banner and returns → PASS

**Test 2**: `require_triage_current_blocks_review_resolve_while_pending`
- patterns = ["review::new"] → matches review::new with detector="review" (IS review)
- pending_behind_objective_backlog = TRUE
- matched_targets = [review::new issue]
- _targets_include_review_work([review::new]) = TRUE
- Falls through to error case
- Raises CommandError with "triage is pending behind the current objective backlog" → PASS

Perfect! The logic is correct. Now let me check the plan passing logic. Looking at the resolve/cmd.py change:

```python
require_triage_current_or_exit(
    state=state,
    plan=plan_access.plan if isinstance(plan_access.plan, dict) and not plan_access.degraded else None,
    patterns=args.patterns,
    ...
)
```

This passes the plan only if it's a valid dict and not degraded. This is good because it avoids passing garbage data to the guardrail.

```
+def _matched_open_targets(state: dict, patterns: list[str]) -> list[dict]:
+    matched_by_id: dict[str, dict] = {}
+    for pattern in patterns:
+        for issue in state_mod.match_issues(state, pattern, status_filter="open"):
+            issue_id = str(issue.get("id", "")).strip()
+            if issue_id and issue_id not in matched_by_id:
+                matched_by_id[issue_id] = issue
+    return list(matched_by_id.values())
+
+
+def _targets_include_review_work(matched_targets: list[dict]) -> bool:
+    return any(issue.get("detector") in _REVIEW_DETECTORS for issue in matched_targets)
+
+
 __all__ = [
     "TriageGuardrailResult",
     "print_triage_guardrail_info",
```

This is a minor wording change that's fine - makes it more consistent with the new "pending" terminology.

Now let me check for one important consistency issue - the test shows that when patterns match non-review work while pending, it should print the banner. But I need to make sure that the banner being printed doesn't have contradictory information. Let me trace through that code path one more time:

```python
if result.pending_behind_objective_backlog and patterns:
    matched_targets = _matched_open_targets(state, patterns)
    if matched_targets and not _targets_include_review_work(matched_targets):
        banner = triage_phase_banner(
            result._plan or {},
            state,
            snapshot=result._snapshot,
        )
        if banner:
            print(colorize(f"  {banner}", "yellow"))
        return
```

So when resolving obj::1 (non-review work), it:
1. Checks pending_behind_objective_backlog = TRUE
2. Gets matched_targets = [obj::1]
3. Checks _targets_include_review_work = FALSE
4. Gets banner from triage_phase_banner with the deferred logic
5. Prints the banner as yellow info
6. Returns (allows the resolve to proceed)

This seems correct. The banner should say something like "TRIAGE PENDING — review issues changed since last triage and will activate after objective work is complete."

Let me verify one more potential issue - what if `result._plan` is None when we try to get the banner? Looking at line in guardrails.py:

```python
    snapshot=result._snapshot,
)
```

```
def ensure_plan_defaults(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Normalize a loaded plan to ensure all keys exist.

    Runtime contract is v8-only. Legacy payloads are upgraded in-place once.
    """
    defaults = empty_plan()
    for key, value in defaults.items():
        plan.setdefault(key, value)
    _upgrade_plan_to_v8(plan)
    subjective_defer_meta = plan.get("subjective_defer_meta")
    if isinstance(subjective_defer_meta, dict):
        subjective_defer_meta.pop("force_visible_ids", None)
    epic_triage_meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta")
    if isinstance(epic_triage_meta, dict):
        epic_triage_meta.pop("triage_force_visible", None)
```

Good. So Rust uses `tool_phase_clippy()` which runs `cargo clippy`. Now, the key insight is that the GitHub issue says:

1. Stale deleted files persist in state after rescan
2. `cargo clippy` reports clean (no unused imports), but Desloppify reports them as "unused"

This suggests that maybe Rust is reporting "unused" issues from somewhere else (perhaps clippy is being parsed for "unused-*" warnings). Let me check how clippy messages are parsed:

> AGENT

Perfect! I can see that `parse_clippy_messages` is used and it parses compiler warnings/messages, including "unused variable" messages. So Rust IS using clippy to report "unused" style issues. The problem is that:

1. When files are deleted, the old issues persist in state
2. When a re-scan happens, those old deleted files' issues don't get cleaned up
3. The `show orphaned --status open` returns nothing because the old issues have somehow gotten a different status (maybe they were auto-resolved but still in state?)

> AGENT

Good, `ensure_plan_defaults` uses `setdefault`, so passing an empty dict is fine - it will just populate it with defaults.

Now let me look at one more potential issue. In the require_triage_current_or_exit function, when we have pending_behind_objective_backlog, the error message says:

```
"BLOCKED: review issues changed since the last triage, but triage is pending"
" behind the current objective backlog."
```

But what if there are NO new_ids? Let me check:

Looking at the code flow:
```python
new_ids = result.new_ids
if result.pending_behind_objective_backlog:
    lines = [...]
    raise CommandError(...)
```

So the error is raised before checking `new_ids`. This means even if there are no new IDs, it will still error. Is that correct?

Actually, looking at the detection logic again:

```python
pending_behind_objective_backlog = (
    not snapshot.has_triage_in_queue
    and bool(resolved_state)
    and is_mid_cycle(resolved_plan)
    and has_objective_backlog(resolved_state, None)
)
```

This doesn't check `is_triage_stale`. Wait, let me re-read the PR diff for the guardrails.py change:

Looking at the full condition:

```python
if not snapshot.is_triage_stale:
    return TriageGuardrailResult(_plan=resolved_plan, _snapshot=snapshot)

pending_behind_objective_backlog = (
    not snapshot.has_triage_in_queue
    and bool(resolved_state)
    and is_mid_cycle(resolved_plan)
    and has_objective_backlog(resolved_state, None)
)

return TriageGuardrailResult(
    is_stale=True,
    pending_behind_objective_backlog=pending_behind_objective_backlog,
    ...
)
```

Ah! I see - the `pending_behind_objective_backlog` is computed ONLY when `is_triage_stale` is TRUE. Because the early return happens if triage is NOT stale. So the logic is:

1. If triage is NOT stale → return early (not stale)
2. Triage IS stale, so compute pending_behind_objective_backlog
3. Return with is_stale=True and pending_behind_objective_backlog=(computed value)

This is correct! So pending_behind_objective_backlog can only be TRUE when is_triage_stale is also TRUE, which means there ARE new_ids.

Great! Now let me look at the test expectations one more time to make sure everything is consistent. Looking at `test_triage_guardrail_messages_use_pending_copy_when_triage_is_deferred`:

```python
def test_triage_guardrail_messages_use_pending_copy_when_triage_is_deferred() -> None:
    messages = triage_guardrail_messages(
        plan=_pending_triage_plan(),
        state=_pending_triage_state(),
    )

    assert any("activate after the current objective backlog is clear" in msg for msg in messages)
    assert any(msg.startswith("TRIAGE PENDING") for msg in messages)
    assert not any("Run the staged triage runner" in msg for msg in messages)
```

So the test expects:
1. A message containing "activate after the current objective backlog is clear" - ✓ from the new message in triage_guardrail_messages
2. A message starting with "TRIAGE PENDING" - ✓ from triage_phase_banner 
3. NO message containing "Run the staged triage runner" - ✓ because it uses the new pending message instead

```
   197→
   198→def _render_terminal_queue_view(
   199→    *,
   200→    queue: dict,
   201→    items: list[dict],
   202→    state: dict,
   203→    opts: NextOptions,
   204→    plan_for_queue: dict,
   205→    plan_data: dict | None,
   206→    effective_cluster: str | None,
   207→    target_strict: float,
   208→    ctx,
   209→    show_plan_context: bool,
   210→    show_execution_prompt: bool,
   211→) -> None:
   212→    """Render terminal output for a non-empty queue."""
   213→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   214→    issues_scoped = path_scoped_issues(
   215→        (state.get("work_items") or state.get("issues", {})),
   216→        state.get("scan_path"),
   217→    )
   218→    plan_start_strict = None
   219→    breakdown = None
   220→    if show_plan_context:
   221→        plan_start_strict, breakdown = _plan_queue_context(
   222→            state=state,
   223→            plan_data=plan_for_queue,
   224→            context=ctx,
   225→        )
   226→    queue_total = breakdown.queue_total if breakdown else 0
   227→
   228→    _render_queue_header(queue, opts.explain)
   229→    strict_score = score_snapshot(state).strict
   230→    if _show_empty_queue(
   231→        queue,
   232→        strict_score,
   233→        plan_start_strict=plan_start_strict,
   234→        target_strict=target_strict,
   235→    ):
   236→        return
   237→
   238→    potentials = _merge_potentials_safe(state.get("potentials", {}))
   239→    next_render_mod.render_terminal_items(
   240→        items,
   241→        dim_scores,
   242→        issues_scoped,
   243→        group=opts.group,
   244→        explain=opts.explain,
   245→        potentials=potentials,
   246→        plan=plan_data,
   247→        cluster_filter=effective_cluster,
   248→    )
   249→    next_nudges_mod.render_single_item_resolution_hint(items)
   250→    if show_plan_context:
   251→        next_nudges_mod.render_uncommitted_reminder(plan_data)
   252→        next_nudges_mod.render_followup_nudges(
   253→            state,
   254→            dim_scores,
   255→            issues_scoped,
   256→            strict_score=strict_score,
   257→            target_strict_score=target_strict,
   258→            queue_total=queue_total,
   259→            plan_start_strict=plan_start_strict,
   260→            breakdown=breakdown,
   261→        )
   262→    print()
   263→
   264→    if items and plan_data and show_execution_prompt:
   265→        print_user_message(
   266→            "Start working on the task above. When done:"
   267→            " `desloppify plan resolve`. Full queue:"
   268→            " `desloppify plan show`."
   269→        )
   270→
   271→
   272→def _plan_data_for_view(plan: dict, *, show_plan_context: bool) -> dict | None:
   273→    if not show_plan_context:
   274→        return None
   275→    if plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("overrides") or plan.get("clusters"):
   276→        return plan
   277→    return None
   278→
   279→
   280→def _resolve_queue_items(
   281→    *,
   282→    state: dict,
   283→    config: dict,
   284→    opts: NextOptions,
   285→    plan_for_queue: dict,
   286→    target_strict: float,
   287→    build_work_queue_fn,
   288→) -> tuple[object, dict, list[dict], str | None]:
   289→    ctx = queue_context(
   290→        state,
   291→        target_strict=target_strict,
   292→        config=config,
   293→        plan=plan_for_queue,
   294→    )
   295→    effective_cluster = _resolve_cluster_focus(
   296→        plan_for_queue,
   297→        cluster_arg=opts.cluster,
   298→        scope=opts.scope,
   299→    )
   300→    queue = build_work_queue_fn(
   301→        state,
   302→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
   303→            count=None,
   304→            scope=opts.scope,
   305→            status=opts.status,
   306→            include_subjective=True,
   307→            subjective_threshold=target_strict,
   308→            explain=opts.explain,
   309→            include_skipped=opts.include_skipped,
   310→            context=ctx,
   311→        ),
   312→    )
   313→    return ctx, queue, queue.get("items", []), effective_cluster
   314→
   315→
   316→def _apply_queue_view_transforms(
   317→    *,
   318→    items: list[dict],
   319→    queue: dict,
   320→    opts: NextOptions,
   321→    plan_for_queue: dict,
   322→    effective_cluster: str | None,
   323→    collapse_plan_clusters: bool,
   324→) -> list[dict]:
   325→    visible = items
   326→    if collapse_plan_clusters and effective_cluster and plan_for_queue:
   327→        visible = filter_cluster_focus(visible, plan_for_queue, effective_cluster)
   328→    elif (
   329→        collapse_plan_clusters
   330→        and plan_for_queue
   331→        and not effective_cluster
   332→        and not plan_for_queue.get("active_cluster")
   333→    ):
   334→        visible = collapse_clusters(visible, plan_for_queue)
   335→
   336→    if opts.count:
   337→        visible = visible[: opts.count]
   338→        queue["items"] = visible
   339→        queue["total"] = len(visible)
   340→    return visible
   341→
   342→
   343→def _render_non_empty_queue(
   344→    *,
   345→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   346→    state: dict,
   347→    items: list[dict],
   348→    queue: dict,
   349→    plan_data: dict | None,
   350→    guardrail_warnings: list[str],
   351→    write_query_fn,
   352→    view: QueueViewConfig,
   353→    opts: NextOptions,
   354→    plan_for_queue: dict,
   355→    effective_cluster: str | None,
   356→    target_strict: float,
   357→    ctx,
   358→    resolve_lang_fn,
   359→) -> None:
   360→    lang = resolve_lang_fn(args)
   361→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   362→    narrative = compute_narrative(
   363→        state,
   364→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command=view.command_name, plan=plan_data),
   365→    )
   366→    payload = _write_next_payload(
   367→        queue=queue,

   369→        state=state,

   372→        guardrail_warnings=guardrail_warnings,
   373→        write_query_fn=write_query_fn,
   374→        command_name=view.command_name,
   375→    )
   376→
   377→    if _emit_requested_output(opts, payload, items, command_name=view.command_name):

   379→
   380→    _render_terminal_queue_view(
   381→        queue=queue,
   382→        items=items,
   383→        state=state,
   384→        opts=opts,
   385→        plan_for_queue=plan_for_queue,
   386→        plan_data=plan_data,
   387→        effective_cluster=effective_cluster,
   388→        target_strict=target_strict,
   389→        ctx=ctx,
   390→        show_plan_context=view.show_plan_context,
   391→        show_execution_prompt=view.show_execution_prompt,
   392→    )
   393→
   394→
   395→def _build_and_render_queue_view(
```

Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the issue. Let me create a summary document:

## GitHub Issue #412 Investigation Summary

### **Problem 1: Stale Deleted Files Not Pruned from State**

**Root Cause:**
The scan merge pipeline does NOT automatically delete issues for files that no longer exist. Instead, it only marks them as "scan-verified" if they were previously manually resolved (status = "wontfix", "fixed", or "false_positive"). **Open issues from deleted files persist in state indefinitely.**

**Evidence:**
- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/merge_issues.py` (lines 74-153)
  - Function `verify_disappeared()` handles auto-resolution of disappeared issues
  - Line 136-137: **CRITICAL** - If `previous_status == "open"`, the function SKIPS marking it verified and continues. It never deletes the issue.
  - Lines 138-151: Only issues with status in ("wontfix", "fixed", "false_positive") get marked as scan-verified when absent
  - **No deletion occurs** - issues remain in state with "open" status

**Process Flow:**
1. User deletes/renames a Rust file (e.g., `src/deleted.rs`)
2. Next scan runs via `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/workflow.py` (line 410)
3. `merge_scan()` is called which invokes `upsert_issues()` and `verify_disappeared()`
4. Issues from `deleted.rs` are NOT in current scan results
5. `verify_disappeared()` sees old issues are gone but their status is "open"
6. Function returns early (line 137) without modifying the issue
7. **Issue remains in `state["work_items"]` with "open" status**

**Consequence:**
- State accumulates stale issues
- These stale issues have file paths pointing to files that no longer exist
- When showing issues or building work queue, they cause unexpected behavior

---

### **Problem 2: Rust "Unused Import" False Positives from Stale Files**

**Root Cause:**
Rust uses `cargo clippy` (via `tool_phase_clippy()`) to detect unused imports and variables:

- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/rust/phases.py` (lines 273-279)
  - `tool_phase_clippy()` runs clippy warnings at the "clippy_warning" detector level
  - Message parser: `parse_clippy_messages()` extracts "unused variable", "unused import" etc. warnings from JSON output

**The Conflict:**
1. Clippy is **per-file analysis** within current repo state
2. When a file is deleted, clippy no longer analyzes it → no "unused" warnings
3. But stale issues from that deleted file persist in `state-rust.json`
4. **User runs `cargo clippy` manually**: sees no errors (file is gone)
5. **But desloppify scan** shows stale "unused" issues in state that were never auto-resolved

**Evidence:**
- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/rust/tests/test_tools.py`
  - Test at line ~100: `test_parse_clippy_messages_ignores_non_json_noise()` shows clippy output is parsed for warnings
  - Warnings like "unused variable: `name`" are extracted and become issues
- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/rust/tools.py`
  - `parse_clippy_messages()` extracts all warning-level messages including unused checks

**Missing:** There is NO Rust-specific "unused import" detector. Unlike Python and TypeScript which have dedicated `unused.py` detectors, Rust relies entirely on clippy parsing for unused issues.

---

### **Problem 3: `show orphaned --status open` Returns Nothing**

**Root Cause:**
The `show` command filters by file **scope** and may exclude files that no longer exist in the filesystem.

**Evidence:**
- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/scope.py` (lines 8-29)
  - `path_scoped_issues()` and `issue_in_scan_scope()` filter issues by file path prefix matching
  - Line 22-29: Issues are only included if their file path matches the scan scope
  - **BUT:** This filtering happens DURING queue building, not state inspection
  
- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/show/scope.py` (lines 193-211)
  - `load_matches()` calls `build_work_queue()` which applies scope/status filters
  - This is AFTER state load, so stale issues may be filtered by path scope

**Possible Behavior:**
If stale issues from deleted files are:
1. Outside current scan path → filtered out by scope
2. Or have path that doesn't match current project structure → filtered by file existence checks

Then `show orphaned --status open` would find nothing even though issues exist in state.

---

### **Associated Changes (Git Status)**

The deletion of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/rust/detectors/custom.py` (shown as `D desloppify/languages/rust/detectors/custom.py` in git status) is a **compatibility cleanup** - it was just a re-export wrapper for detector imports. This is NOT related to the stale files problem, but it's contemporaneous with the Rust plugin maturation.

---

### **Summary of Root Issues**

| Issue | Location | Root Cause |
|-------|----------|-----------|
| **Stale files persist** | `merge_issues.py:136-137` | Open issues from deleted files are never auto-resolved or deleted |
| **Unused import false positives** | `merge_issues.py` + `phases.py` | Stale issues from deleted files remain with "open" status; never cleaned |
| **show orphaned --status open finds nothing** | `scope.py`, `selection.py` | Queue filtering by scope/status excludes stale issues from deleted files |

---

### **Missing Features**

1. **No automatic pruning of deleted files' issues** - The `verify_disappeared()` function skips open issues, leaving them orphaned in state
2. **No file existence check on rescan** - Issues are never validated against actual filesystem
3. **No "stale file issue" detector** - Unlike other languages, Rust could benefit from a detector that flags issues with files that no longer exist

```
    58→    return state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    59→
    60→
    61→def _preview_resolve_count(state: dict, patterns: list[str]) -> int:
    62→    """Count unique open issues matching the provided patterns."""
    63→    matched_ids: set[str] = set()
    64→    for pattern in patterns:
    65→        for issue in state_mod.match_issues(state, pattern, status_filter="open"):
    66→            issue_id = issue.get("id")
    67→            if issue_id:
    68→                matched_ids.add(issue_id)
    69→    return len(matched_ids)
    70→
    71→
    72→def _estimate_wontfix_strict_delta(
    73→    state: dict,
    74→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    75→    *,
    76→    attestation: str | None,
    77→    resolve_all_patterns_fn,
    78→) -> float:
    79→    """Estimate strict score drop if this resolve command is applied as wontfix."""
    80→    before = state_mod.score_snapshot(state).strict
    81→    if before is None:
    82→        return 0.0
    83→
    84→    preview_state = copy.deepcopy(state)
    85→    resolve_all_patterns_fn(preview_state, args, attestation=attestation)
    86→    after = state_mod.score_snapshot(preview_state).strict
    87→    if after is None:
    88→        return 0.0
    89→    return max(0.0, before - after)
    90→
    91→
    92→def _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation(
    93→    state: dict,
    94→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    95→    *,
    96→    attestation: str | None,
    97→    resolve_all_patterns_fn,
    98→) -> None:
    99→    if args.status != "wontfix":
   100→        return
   101→
   102→    preview_count = _preview_resolve_count(state, args.patterns)
   103→    if preview_count <= 10:
   104→        return
   105→    if getattr(args, "confirm_batch_wontfix", False):
   106→        return
   107→
   108→    strict_delta = _estimate_wontfix_strict_delta(
   109→        state,
   110→        args,
   111→        attestation=attestation,
   112→        resolve_all_patterns_fn=resolve_all_patterns_fn,
   113→    )
   114→    _emit_warning(f"Large wontfix batch detected ({preview_count} issues).")
   115→    if strict_delta > 0:
   116→        _emit_warning(f"Estimated strict-score debt added now: {strict_delta:.1f} points.")
   117→    raise CommandError(
   118→        "Re-run with --confirm-batch-wontfix if this debt is intentional."
   119→    )
   120→
```

## REVIEW COMPLETE

I've thoroughly reviewed PR #413 "Fix planner deadlock when triage goes stale mid-cycle". Here are my findings:

### Summary of Changes

The PR fixes a deadlock where `plan resolve --confirm` and `plan triage --run-stages` were both blocked when triage was stale but deferred behind objective backlog. The fix allows objective resolves to continue while keeping direct review resolves blocked.

### Changes Made

**1. Core Logic in guardrails.py:**
- Added `pending_behind_objective_backlog: bool` field to `TriageGuardrailResult` to distinguish between "completely blocked" and "pending behind objectives"
- New detection logic computes this when: triage is stale + not in queue + state exists + mid-cycle + objective backlog exists
- Updated `require_triage_current_or_exit()` to accept optional `plan` and `patterns` parameters
- Added conditional pass-through: if patterns match ONLY non-review issues (objective work), the resolve is allowed with a yellow info banner
- If patterns match review issues, the resolve is blocked with an error

**2. Helper functions:**
- `_matched_open_targets()`: Uses `state.match_issues()` to find matching issues
- `_targets_include_review_work()`: Checks if matched targets are review/concerns detectors

**3. Banner updates:**
- `triage_phase_banner()`: New condition shows "TRIAGE PENDING — review issues changed since last triage and will activate after objective work is complete"
- `lifecycle.py`: Updated message from "Cannot start triage while objective backlog is still open" to "Triage is pending behind the current objective backlog" (more consistent terminology)

**4. Message updates:**
- When pending: "new review issue(s) arrived since the last triage. They will activate after the current objective backlog is clear"
- When not pending: "new review issue(s) not yet triaged. Run the staged triage runner..."

**5. Resolve command:**
- Updated call to pass `plan` (if available and not degraded) and `patterns` to guardrail

**6. Tests:**
- 4 new tests in `test_guardrails.py` covering status detection, message content, and selective blocking
- 1 new test in `test_triage_phase_banner.py` covering deferred banner

### Correctness Analysis

**✓ Logic is sound:**
- Detection condition correctly uses all-AND logic: only marks pending when ALL conditions met
- Conditional flow correctly routes objective vs review work differently
- Message copy is semantically consistent across all surfaces
- Banner appears in correct position (before undispositioned check)

**✓ Edge cases handled:**
- Empty patterns: Guarded with `and patterns` check
- None plan: Guarded in resolve/cmd.py before passing
- Degraded plan: Explicitly excluded from being passed
- No objective backlog: pending flag stays False
- Not mid-cycle: pending flag stays False
- Triage not stale: Early return before pending computation

**✓ Backward compatible:**
- New function parameters have defaults (None)
- New dataclass field has default (False)
- Existing callers continue to work unchanged (queue_flow.py unchanged)

**✓ Imports are all available:**
- `has_objective_backlog` and `is_mid_cycle` exist in engine._plan.sync.context
- `state.match_issues` re-exported from state_compat

**✓ Test coverage is adequate:**
- Tests verify correct state detection
- Tests verify correct message selection
- Tests verify objective resolves are allowed
- Tests verify review resolves are blocked
- Banner test verifies new condition works

### Potential Issues Found: NONE

The implementation is correct. No blocking issues, no missing edge cases, no import problems, no test gaps.

### Minor Observations (Not Issues)

1. Slight wording variance in messages ("objective work is complete" vs "objective backlog is clear") - semantically equivalent, acceptable
2. Lifecycle message change improves consistency with new "pending" terminology
3. Test fixtures use "issues" key instead of "work_items" - fine because match_issues handles both

### Files Modified
- desloppify/app/commands/helpers/guardrails.py (core changes)
- desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py (call site update)
- desloppify/engine/plan_triage.py (banner condition)
- desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/lifecycle.py (message)
- desloppify/tests/commands/helpers/test_guardrails.py (new tests)
- desloppify/tests/plan/test_triage_phase_banner.py (new test)
